# Migrating to v6

> Track and review changes to the CoreUI for Bootstrap source files, documentation, and components to help you migrate from v5 to v6.

> **Work in progress.** v6 is under active development on the `v6-dev` branch —
> this page tracks every migration-relevant change as it lands, so nothing here
> is final until a v6 release ships. The **class API keeps its meaning** —
> `.btn-primary`, `.alert-success` and friends render as before. Where the Sass
> or CSS-variable surface does change, the entry below carries a
> Breaking badge.

## v6.0.0 (unreleased)

### Dependencies

- **Popper is gone; `@floating-ui/dom` replaces it.**
  <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  Every positioned component — tooltips, popovers, dropdowns, autocomplete,
  multi-select, and the pickers — now positions with
  [Floating UI](https://floating-ui.com). `@popperjs/core` is no longer a peer
  dependency and the bundle builds (`coreui.bundle.*`) no longer include it.
  What to change:
  - npm: replace `@popperjs/core` with `@floating-ui/dom` in your dependencies.
  - CDN (non-bundle builds): replace the `popper.min.js` script tag with the
    two Floating UI files, in this order: `floating-ui.core.umd.min.js`, then
    `floating-ui.dom.umd.min.js`.
  - The `popperConfig` option (tooltip, popover, dropdown) is now
    `floatingConfig` and receives a Floating UI
    [computePosition](https://floating-ui.com/docs/computePosition) config —
    Popper's `modifiers` array does not translate; use Floating UI middleware.
  - The tip and menu carry `data-coreui-placement` instead of Popper's
    `data-popper-placement`; CSS or JS keying on the old attribute needs the
    new name.
  - Positioning is now asynchronous (`computePosition` is promise-based).
    Code that read the tip's coordinates synchronously right after `show()`
    should wait for the `shown` event.

### Distributed files

- **The `*.rtl.css` builds are gone.**
  <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  One stylesheet now serves both directions, so `dist/css` no longer carries
  `coreui.rtl.css`, `coreui-grid.rtl.css`, `coreui-reboot.rtl.css`,
  `coreui-utilities.rtl.css`, their minified twins, source maps or SRI hashes —
  20 files in all. Everything positional was already written with logical
  properties; the six places that genuinely differ (the off-canvas panel, the
  range slider's label and tooltip, the select and validation arrows, the
  carousel controls, the breadcrumb divider) now emit their mirrored
  declaration from a `:dir(rtl)` rule in the same file, matched on the
  element's own direction rather than on an ancestor. That costs about 500
  bytes gzip, where an RTL reader used to download a second 56&nbsp;kB
  stylesheet.

  **What to change:** drop the `.rtl` from your stylesheet link and keep
  `dir="rtl"` on `<html>`. `$enable-rtl: false` still produces an LTR-only
  build and `$enable-ltr: false` an RTL-only one, so nothing that was possible
  before stops being possible.

- **The Bootstrap theme and the Bootstrap-compatible JavaScript are gone.**
  <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  `dist/css/themes/bootstrap/bootstrap.css`, `dist/js/bootstrap.js`,
  `bootstrap.bundle.js` and `bootstrap.esm.js` no longer ship, with their
  minified twins, source maps and SRI hashes, and neither does
  `scss/themes/bootstrap/bootstrap.scss`. The two halves no longer agreed in
  v6: the JavaScript wrote `data-bs-*` attributes and `--bs-*` properties
  where the stylesheet still read `data-coreui-*` and `--cui-*` ones.

  **What to change:** load `coreui.min.css` and `coreui.bundle.min.js` (or
  `coreui.min.js`), write `data-coreui-*` attributes, listen for
  `*.coreui.*` events, reach the plugins through `coreui` instead of
  `bootstrap`, and set `--cui-*` properties instead of `--bs-*`.

- **`dist/css` gained `base.css` and a stylesheet per component.**
  `base.css` holds the `:root` tokens and Reboot; `css/components/` holds one
  file per component plus `manifest.json`, which records each file's load order
  and the components it needs alongside it. Nothing was taken away —
  `coreui.css` still carries everything — so this is additive, and pages that
  use a few components can now ship a few kilobytes of CSS instead of the whole
  library. See [per-component CSS files](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/getting-started/install/#per-component-css-files).

- **`dist/css` gained `coreui-legacy.css`, and `coreui.css` lost the v5
  spellings it carries.**
  <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  `coreui.css` is the v6 class API only. The deprecated v5 spellings — the
  colour modifiers (`.alert-{color}`, `.btn-{color}`, `.btn-outline-{color}`,
  `.btn-subtle-{color}`, `.btn-ghost-{color}`, `.callout-{color}`,
  `.chip-{color}`, `.list-group-item-{color}`), the form check markup
  (`.form-check`, `.form-check-input`, `.form-check-label`,
  `.form-check-inline`, `.form-switch`, `.form-switch-lg` / `-xl`) and
  `.btn-check` on the input — moved to `coreui-legacy.css`, 3.4&nbsp;kB gzip.
  That takes 2.1&nbsp;kB gzip off `coreui.css` for markup that has already
  moved, and turns the compatibility layer into something you can see on a
  network tab and delete in one line.

  **What to change:** if your markup still uses any of them, add the sheet
  after `coreui.css` (or last, after `base.css` and the component files):

  ```html
  <link href="css/coreui.min.css" rel="stylesheet">
  <link href="css/coreui-legacy.min.css" rel="stylesheet">
  ```

  From Sass, `@use "coreui-legacy";` next to your `@use "coreui";`. Everything
  in it is still removed in v7. See
  [v5 compatibility CSS](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/getting-started/install/#v5-compatibility-css).

- **`$enable-deprecated-classes` and `$enable-deprecated-form-check` are gone.**
  <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  A separate stylesheet answers the question both flags existed for, without a
  recompile: `false` is now "do not load `coreui-legacy.css`". Sass builds that
  set either flag fail with an unknown-variable error — delete the line.

### Browser support

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

**The minimum supported browsers are now Chrome 123, Edge 123, Firefox 129,
Safari 18 and iOS 18.** Safari 18 is set by `color-mix()`: a mix in `oklch` with
an achromatic operand — `transparent`, white, black, the greys, which is how
every translucent and tinted colour in v6 is made — keeps the other colour's
hue only from Safari 18, where WebKit started carrying missing components
forward as the spec requires; Safari 17.x interpolates the hue towards 0° and
renders indigo as pink. Apple ships Safari 18 for macOS 13 and 14 as well, so
the cut only affects browsers left un-updated. `light-dark()` sets the
Chrome/Edge floor;
`@starting-style` and `transition-behavior: allow-discrete` set Firefox 129 (the
Menu's entry animation) and `@property` needs 128 — its `inherits: false`
registrations are what stop the colour and opacity utilities leaking into
descendants, so an older Firefox does not lose an animation, it gets a bug.
`:has()` sets a lower bound of Firefox 121, since validation and focus states on Multi Select,
Autocomplete and the pickers, the sidebar navigation indicators, calendar
ranges and the rating icons are all selected with it — and an unsupported `:has()` invalidates
the entire rule it appears in. This is the floor the v6 color system already required in practice:
theme decisions resolve in the browser via `light-dark()` and `color-mix()`
with no fallback, so on older browsers dark mode did not work regardless of
what the previous `.browserslistrc` claimed (Chrome 60 / Safari 12). v6 makes
the declaration honest and takes the wins: the compiled CSS drops legacy
vendor prefixes (about 8&nbsp;kB gzip off `coreui.min.css`) and the JavaScript
ships modern syntax un-transpiled. If you must support older browsers, stay
on v5.

### JavaScript

v6's JavaScript is written in TypeScript. The published behaviour is unchanged —
the sources were converted module by module against a gate that required the
minified bundles to stay byte-identical — but the package now carries types, and
what it ships from `js/` has changed.

#### Type declarations ship with the package

`@coreui/coreui-pro` now ships `.d.ts` files and points `types` at
`js/dist/index.d.ts`, so TypeScript consumers get the full API without
`@types` packages or hand-written module declarations:

```ts
import { Modal, MultiSelect } from '@coreui/coreui-pro'

const modal = new Modal('#example', { backdrop: 'static', keyboard: false })
const version: string = Modal.VERSION
```

Each component with options also exports its config type (ContextMenu and
Dropdown take Menu's `MenuConfig`), so an options object can be typed and
reused, and takes it in the constructor and
`getOrCreateInstance`, so an option it
does not know is a type error; a picker also takes the options it forwards to
its calendar, its field and its time columns, except those it sets itself. Declarations are generated from
the sources, not maintained by hand, and CI type-checks a consumer file against
the **emitted** declarations, so the shipped types cannot drift from the code.

#### The `js/src` sources ship as TypeScript

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

The package used to ship `js/src/**/*.js`; it now ships `js/src/**/*.ts`. The
documented per-plugin path is unaffected — **`js/dist/*.js` still ships,
UMD-ready**, and that is what the [Webpack](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/guides/webpack/),
[Vite](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/guides/vite/) and [Parcel](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/guides/parcel/)
guides tell you to import:

```js
import Alert from '@coreui/coreui-pro/js/dist/alert'
```

A bundler that reads the package's `exports` now resolves that `import` to
the ES module next to the UMD file, `js/dist/alert.mjs`; a `require()` still
gets `js/dist/alert.js`.

Only imports that reached into the raw sources are affected:

```diff
- import Alert from '@coreui/coreui-pro/js/src/alert.js'
+ import Alert from '@coreui/coreui-pro/js/dist/alert.js'
```

If your toolchain compiles TypeScript, you can import `js/src/*.ts` directly and
get types and tree-shaking together. The per-plugin files are typed as well —
`js/dist/alert.js` ships next to `js/dist/alert.d.ts`, and `js/dist/alert.mjs`
next to `js/dist/alert.d.mts`.

#### `js/index.esm.js` and `js/index.umd.js` are no longer published

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

These two files are the bundler's entry points, and they import the sources —
which are now TypeScript, so the files could not resolve for a consumer anyway.
They are no longer part of the published package. Use the built bundle instead:

```diff
- import { Tooltip } from '@coreui/coreui-pro/js/index.esm.js'
+ import { Tooltip } from '@coreui/coreui-pro/dist/js/coreui.esm.js'
```

or, if you are already resolving the package by name, `import { Tooltip } from
'@coreui/coreui-pro'` — `module` points at the same ESM bundle.

#### Unused date helpers are gone from `js/dist/util`

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

The `util` modules are internal, but the package exports every file under `js/dist`, so a deep import could reach them. These helpers are no longer exported: `js/dist/util/date-range-picker.js` as a whole, `getFirstAvailableDateInRange`, `getSelectableDates`, `createDateFromMonth`, `createDateFromWeek` and `createDateFromYear` from `util/calendar.js`, `getDateSections` from `util/date-sections.js` (now `getSectionLayout`), and `convertTimeToDate`, `formatTimePartials`, `getAmPm`, `isValidTime` and `shouldUseTwoDigitHour` from `util/time.js`. Most had no reader in the library and are gone; `createDateFromYear`, `formatTimePartials` and `shouldUseTwoDigitHour` stay as module internals. Pass dates to the components in the formats their options take (`startDate: '2026W03'` with `selectionType: 'week'`), or keep a copy of the helper in your code. The nine per-unit comparisons of `util/calendar.js`, `isMonthDisabled`, `isMonthInRange`, `isMonthSelected` and their `isQuarter…` and `isYear…` counterparts, are three functions that take the calendar view as the second argument: `isPeriodDisabled(date, 'months', min, max, disabledDates)`, `isPeriodInRange(date, 'quarters', start, end)` and `isPeriodSelected(date, 'years', start, end)`.

#### `CalendarUtil` and `RangeUtil` in the per-file UMD builds

Loaded with a plain `<script>`, `js/dist/util/calendar.js` registers as `CalendarUtil`, and the new `js/dist/util/range.js` as `RangeUtil`. The calendar util used the `Calendar` global of the component, so the two files overwrote each other and `js/dist/calendar.js` read its helpers from itself. Load the util file before the component, as with the other `js/dist/util` files.

#### `coreui.Offcanvas` in the UMD bundle

The UMD bundle exposed the offcanvas plugin as `coreui.OffCanvas`, while the
ESM bundle, the per-plugin file and the docs all call it `Offcanvas`. The UMD
global now carries `coreui.Offcanvas` as well; `coreui.OffCanvas` stays as an
alias of the same class, so existing scripts keep working:

```diff
- const offcanvas = new coreui.OffCanvas('#offcanvasExample')
+ const offcanvas = new coreui.Offcanvas('#offcanvasExample')
```

#### The UMD bundle keeps what companion packages put on `window.coreui`

`coreui.js` and `coreui.bundle.js` used to replace `window.coreui` with a
fresh object, so `coreui.Utils`, `coreui.ChartJS`, `coreui.DataGrid` or
`coreui.Scheduler` loaded before the library were gone once it loaded. The
library now copies the entries of an existing plain `window.coreui` into its
own object, so the order of the script tags no longer matters. Anything else
under that name, such as an element with `id="coreui"`, is still replaced.

#### One jQuery dispatcher for every plugin

Every `$(el).plugin('method')` call now goes through the same dispatcher:
the instance is created or reused, a string argument names a **public**
method, and anything else throws `TypeError: No method named "…"`. A few
plugins used to let a private name through (`$(el).tooltip('_getConfig')`
called it) or skipped the check altogether (`button`, `number-input`,
`password-input`); they now reject those names like the rest.

#### The attribute that enables a plugin

`data-coreui-toggle="…"` now means one thing only: **this element is a control
the user activates.** It stays on the `<button>`, `<a>` or `<input>` that opens,
closes or presses something — Modal, Offcanvas, Dialog, Drawer, Collapse, Tab,
Tooltip, Popover, Context Menu, Combobox, Button, Loading Button.

Everywhere else the element is a mount point or a source: the plugin builds the
component inside or around it, and nothing is toggled. Those take a bare
`data-coreui-<name>`, the shape Chip Input, Chip Set, Multi Select and the
masked date and time fields already used. Renamed:

```diff
- <div data-coreui-toggle="date-picker"></div>
+ <div data-coreui-date-picker></div>
```

| was | is |
| --- | --- |
| `data-coreui-toggle="autocomplete"` | `data-coreui-autocomplete` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="calendar"` | `data-coreui-calendar` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="date-picker"` | `data-coreui-date-picker` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="date-range-picker"` | `data-coreui-date-range-picker` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="list-box"` | `data-coreui-list-box` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="number-input"` | `data-coreui-number-input` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="otp"` | `data-coreui-otp` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="password-input"` | `data-coreui-password-input` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="password-strength"` | `data-coreui-password-strength` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="range-slider"` | `data-coreui-range-slider` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="rating"` | `data-coreui-rating` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="stepper"` | `data-coreui-stepper` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="time-picker"` | `data-coreui-time-picker` |
| `data-coreui-toggle="transfer"` | `data-coreui-transfer` |

The JavaScript constructors are unchanged — this is the markup entry point
only, and only in the vanilla package.

**`data-coreui="navigation"` is gone.** The sidebar navigation read two
attributes for one job; the v4-era spelling was kept for compatibility and is
now dropped. Use `data-coreui-navigation`, which v5 already documented as the
one to write.

#### Lifecycle methods return a promise

`show()`, `hide()`, `toggle()` and `close()` returned before the transition
ended, so continuing after one meant listening for `shown.coreui.*` /
`hidden.coreui.*`. They now return a promise that resolves at the same moment
that event fires, so you can `await` the method instead:

```diff
- myCollapseEl.addEventListener('shown.coreui.collapse', () => {
-   // the area is expanded
- })
- collapse.show()
+ await collapse.show()
+ // the area is expanded
```

Collapse, Menu, Modal, Offcanvas, Popover, Sidebar and Tooltip return one from
`show`/`hide`/`toggle`, Toast from `show`/`hide`, Tab from `show`, and Alert
from `close`. The event path is unchanged and still the right tool when you must
react to changes your own code did not start.

The promise always settles — a call that does nothing (already open, a prevented
`show.coreui.*`, an instance disposed mid-transition) resolves right away rather
than stranding the caller. It resolves with no value, so it tells you the call
finished, not whether the state changed.

#### Live changes go through setters, not `update(config)`

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

`update()` meant two different things. On Combobox, List box, Transfer, Menu,
Progress, Range, Tooltip and Popup it takes no argument and recomputes from what
the component already owns; on Autocomplete, Calendar, MultiSelect, OTP Input,
Range Slider, Rating and the date/time inputs it took an options object and
replaced the configuration. Calling it the first way on a component that meant
the second one threw a `TypeError`.

Options are now fixed once a component starts. What can change on a live
instance, its value, its data and its state, has a method of its own, named
after the option it changes:

```diff
- multiSelect.update({ options })
+ multiSelect.setOptions(options)
- rating.update({ value: 2 })
+ rating.setValue(2)
- dateInput.update({ validationState: 'invalid' })
+ dateInput.setValidationState('invalid')
- autocomplete.update({ disabled: true })
+ autocomplete.disable()
```

| Component | Methods |
| --- | --- |
| Autocomplete | `setValue()`, `setOptions()`, `setValidationState()`, `disable()`, `enable()` |
| Calendar | `setStartDate()`, `setEndDate()`, `setRange()`, `setCalendarDate()`, `setSelectEndDate()` |
| Chip Input, Combobox | `setValidationState()`; Combobox keeps `setValue()` and `setItems()` |
| Date Input, Time Input | `setDate()`, `setValidationState()`, `disable()`, `enable()` |
| Date Range Input, Date Range Picker | `setStartDate()`, `setEndDate()`, `setRange()`, `setValidationState()` |
| Date Picker, Time Picker | `setDate()` / `setTime()`, `setValidationState()` |
| Multi Select | `setValue()`, `setOptions()`, `setTotal()`, `setValidationState()`, `disable()`, `enable()` |
| OTP Input, Range Slider, Rating | `setValue()`, `setValidationState()`, `disable()`, `enable()` |

- **A setter takes the type of its option.** A wrong type throws the
  `TypeError` the constructor throws, and the component is left as it was;
  Autocomplete and MultiSelect skipped that check on `update()`.
- **The date and time setters fire no change event**, since the code that
  calls them already holds the value. `setDate()` of Date Input, Time Input and
  Date Picker, `setTime()` of Time Picker, `setStartDate()`, `setEndDate()`
  and `setRange()` of Date Range Input, Date Range Picker and Calendar, and
  `setCalendarDate()` and `setSelectEndDate()` of Calendar change the value
  without `dateChange`, `timeChange`, `startDateChange`, `endDateChange`,
  `calendarDateChange`, `selectEndChange` or the native `input` and `change` of
  the value field;
  `errorChange` still reports a date the field refuses. The verbs (`clear()`,
  `reset()`, `today()`, `now()`) and what the user does fire as before.
  `setDate(date, true)` of Date Input and Time Input and the same last argument
  of the Date Range Input setters fire the events typing the date would, for a date
  the user gives through another control.
- **Other options are fixed.** A new format, size, label, icon, `name`,
  `required` or `readonly` means disposing the instance and creating a new one.
  `update()` applied some of them, such as `format` on Date Input or `size` on
  Rating, and ignored the rest in silence on Combobox, Chip Input, Date Range
  Input and the pickers.
- **A setter keeps what it does not set.** `setValue()` keeps a given
  validation state, `setValidationState()` keeps the value, and Rating and
  Range Slider no longer fall back to the defaults: `rating.setValue(2)` on a
  rating built with `{ itemCount: 3 }` keeps three items.
- **The setters are reachable the same way everywhere**, including through the
  static and jQuery dispatchers, which pass the method name and whatever
  follows it: `coreui.Rating.ratingInterface(el, 'setValue', 2)`.

`Toaster.update(id, options)` is unchanged — it rewrites one toast in the store
rather than reconfiguring the component.

#### A getter says what it returns

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

`getSelected()` returned three different things. List box gave the selected
values, Chip Set gave the selected **elements** while `getSelectedValues()` sat
next to it giving the values, and Transfer required an argument no other
component takes:

```js
chipSet.getSelected().join(',') // [object HTMLDivElement],[object HTMLDivElement]
transfer.getSelected()          // TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined
```

Every getter now carries the noun it returns:

```diff
- listBox.getSelected()
+ listBox.getSelectedValues()

- chipSet.getSelected()
+ chipSet.getSelectedValues()

- transfer.getSelected('source')
+ transfer.getSelectedValues('source')
```

Chip Set's `getValues()` and `getSelectedValues()` are unchanged and are now
the whole selection API: the element list behind them is internal. A chip
carries a value of its own — `data-coreui-chip-value`, falling back to the
chip's text — so values, not nodes, are what the rest of the plugin works
with.

Transfer still takes `'source'` or `'target'`, the argument its
`setLoading(loading, list)` already takes. Anything else now throws a
`TypeError` naming the two lists instead of failing on an undefined property.

#### The button that opens a panel is `pickerIcon`

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

`indicator` named two unrelated things. On Autocomplete it was a boolean that
showed the button opening the options menu; on List box, Combobox and Transfer
it is a string choosing the marker drawn on each option. The button also
answered to three different names across the family — `indicator`,
`indicatorIcon`, and `ariaIndicatorLabel` next to `ariaToggleLabel`.

The word stays where it describes something that indicates — the per-option
marker — and the button takes the name the platform gives it
([`::picker-icon`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Selectors/::picker-icon),
`::picker(select)`, `showPicker()`):

```diff
- new coreui.Autocomplete(el, { indicator: true })
+ new coreui.Autocomplete(el, { pickerIcon: true })

- new coreui.DatePicker(el, { indicatorIcon: mySvg, ariaToggleLabel: 'Open' })
+ new coreui.DatePicker(el, { pickerIcon: mySvg, ariaPickerLabel: 'Open' })

- new coreui.MultiSelect(el, { optionsStyle: 'text' })
+ new coreui.MultiSelect(el, { indicator: 'none' })
```

| was | is | on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `indicator: boolean` | `pickerIcon: string \| boolean` | Autocomplete |
| `indicatorIcon` | `pickerIcon` | the four pickers |
| `ariaIndicatorLabel` | `ariaPickerLabel` | Autocomplete, Multi Select |
| `ariaToggleLabel` | `ariaPickerLabel` | the four pickers |
| `optionsStyle: 'checkbox' \| 'text'` | `indicator: 'checkbox' \| 'none'` | Multi Select |

`pickerIcon` takes over both jobs the old pair did: `false` renders no button,
`true` renders the built-in icon, and a string renders your own inline SVG
(sanitized). Autocomplete and Multi Select gained it — their icon used to be
hard-coded — so `pickerIcon: false` now hides Multi Select's button too.

**Unchanged:** `indicator` on List box, Combobox and Transfer, along with the
`.list-box-option-indicator` class and the `data-coreui-indicator` attribute;
Password Input's `ariaToggleLabel`, because that button toggles visibility
rather than opening a picker; and the shared `.form-control-action` class,
which eight components use for three different jobs.

#### The "N selected" text is one option, and it can be a function

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

Four components rendered the same sentence under three option names and two
shapes: Combobox substituted `{count}` into `selectedText`, List box and
Transfer appended `selectedCounterText` after `count/total`, and Multi Select
appended `selectionTypeCounterText` after the count.

Appending cannot be translated. A translator receives only the tail of the
sentence, so the number cannot move and the noun cannot be inflected — Polish
needs "2 zaznaczone", "5 zaznaczonych" and "1 zaznaczony" from the same option,
and a language that puts the number last cannot be served at all.

```diff
- new coreui.ListBox(el, { selectedCounterText: 'selected' })
+ new coreui.ListBox(el, { selectedLabel: (count, total) => `${count}/${total} selected` })

- new coreui.Combobox(el, { selectedText: '{count} selected' })
+ new coreui.Combobox(el, { selectedLabel: (count) => `${count} selected` })

- new coreui.MultiSelect(el, { selectionTypeCounterText: 'item(s) selected' })
+ new coreui.MultiSelect(el, { selectedLabel: (count) => `${count} item(s) selected` })
```

| was | is |
| --- | --- |
| `selectedText` (Combobox) | `selectedLabel` |
| `selectedCounterText` (List box, Transfer) | `selectedLabel` |
| `selectionTypeCounterText` (Multi Select) | `selectedLabel` |

**The string form stays**, because a function cannot be written into
`data-coreui-selected-label`: `{count}` and `{total}` are replaced in it, so
markup-only pages keep a translatable counter.

The argument order matches the two function options the library already ships —
`Rating.ariaLabel(value, itemCount)` and `OTPInput.ariaLabel(index, total)`.
`total` is passed everywhere, including the two components whose default text
ignores it, so one function can serve all four.

#### One selection event, one payload key

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

Seven components told you the selection had changed, under two event names and
four payload keys. Autocomplete and Multi Select fired `changed`, everyone else
fired `change`; the values arrived as `value`, `selected`, `values`, or as the
pair `sourceValues`/`targetValues`. Nothing announced the difference, so code
moved between two components of the same family read `undefined`:

```js
// Multi Select rewritten as a Combobox — the listener never runs
el.addEventListener('changed.coreui.combobox', event => save(event.value))

// List box — the event fires, the value is undefined
el.addEventListener('change.coreui.list-box', event => save(event.value))
```

The verb is now `change` everywhere and the values arrive as `event.value`:

```diff
- el.addEventListener('changed.coreui.autocomplete', event => save(event.value))
+ el.addEventListener('change.coreui.autocomplete', event => save(event.value))

- el.addEventListener('changed.coreui.multi-select', event => save(event.value))
+ el.addEventListener('change.coreui.multi-select', event => save(event.value))

- el.addEventListener('change.coreui.list-box', event => save(event.selected))
+ el.addEventListener('change.coreui.list-box', event => save(event.value))

- el.addEventListener('change.coreui.chip-set', event => save(event.values))
+ el.addEventListener('change.coreui.chip-set', event => save(event.value))

- el.addEventListener('change.coreui.transfer', event => save(event.targetValues))
+ el.addEventListener('change.coreui.transfer', event => save(event.value))
```

| was | is |
| --- | --- |
| `changed.coreui.autocomplete`, `changed.coreui.multi-select` | `change.coreui.*` |
| `event.selected` (List box) | `event.value` |
| `event.values` (Chip Set, Chip Input) | `event.value` |
| `event.targetValues` (Transfer) | `event.value` |
| `event.sourceValues` (Transfer) | `event.source` |

`event.value` carries what that component's own getter returns, which is the
one thing the key never told you before: `getValue()` for Autocomplete and
Combobox, the selected values for List box (an array even in `single` mode),
the selected options for Multi Select, the chip values for Chip Set and Chip
Input. Transfer's value is its target list, with the source list alongside as
`event.source`; `event.target` stays the `.transfer` element.

Two events keep their own names because they answer a different question.
`select.coreui.chip-set` still carries `selected` — membership and selection are
separate in a chip set — and `move`/`moved.coreui.transfer` still carry
`direction` and the `values` that are moving, which is the change itself rather
than the state after it.

On a page that loads jQuery, the two renamed events now reach `onchange`
handlers written as a DOM property or an inline attribute, on the element or
any ancestor: jQuery's own trigger drops the namespace for that one path.
Measured in Chromium, a `document.onchange` handler goes from zero calls to one
per selected option. The five components that already fired `change` have
always behaved this way. Handlers bound with `$(el).on('change')` are not
affected, and neither is `addEventListener`.

`changed.coreui.range` keeps its name. It is not a selection event: Range
relays the value of the native `<input type="range">` it decorates, on every
native `input` and `change`. Range Slider, the component of this family that
does have a selection, already fires `change`.

#### `announce()` (new)

`announce(message, { context, priority, timeout })` reads a message to screen
reader users through a visually hidden live region of the page, or of the open
modal dialog, native or marked with `aria-modal="true"` like the panel of a
date picker, since a modal dialog leaves the rest of the page inert. `priority`
takes the values of `ariaNotify()`, `'normal'` and `'high'`. Toaster
announces its toasts through it. See
[Accessibility](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/getting-started/accessibility/#announcing-messages).

### Components

#### Accordion on the native `<details>`

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The accordion renders on the native `<details>`/`<summary>` elements now.**
  `.accordion-item` goes on a `<details>` element and `.accordion-header` on its
  `<summary>`; the browser opens and closes the panel, so the component needs no
  initialization and works on markup added after load.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.accordion-button`, `.accordion-collapse` and `.collapsed` are gone.** The
  trigger is the `<summary>` itself and the panel is the element's own content,
  revealed through `::details-content`. Style the open state with
  `.accordion-item[open] > .accordion-header` instead of `:not(.collapsed)`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Grouping moves from `data-coreui-parent` to the `name` attribute.** Give the
  `<details>` elements of one accordion the same `name` to keep a single item
  open at a time; omit it for an always-open accordion. The `id` plumbing
  (`data-coreui-target`, `aria-controls`, `aria-expanded`) is no longer needed —
  the browser exposes the disclosure state on its own.
- **A `<summary>` is not a heading.** Where the accordion divides a page into
  sections, wrap the label in an `<h2>`–`<h6>` inside the summary; the heading
  inherits the summary's typography.
- **The header's focus ring draws 1px inside the item**
  (`--cui-focus-ring-offset: -1px` on `.accordion-header:focus-visible`), so it
  no longer overlaps the borders of adjacent items.
- **The panel animation is CSS-driven** through `interpolate-size:
  allow-keywords`, which only Chromium implements today. `accordion.js` supplies
  the same animation in Firefox and Safari; that part of it goes away, without a
  breaking change, once they ship `calc-size()`. The component's events and
  methods do not depend on it and stay either way.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The accordion's events replace Collapse's.** `shown`/`hidden.coreui.accordion`
  fire on the `.accordion-item` whenever its open state changes, mirroring the
  native `toggle` event but bubbling, so they can be delegated. There is no
  `show`/`hide` counterpart: `<details>` has no cancelable event before the
  state change, so an item is kept shut by cancelling the click on its
  `<summary>` instead of by preventing an event.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The plugin replaces Collapse's, and it is instantiated on the `.accordion`
  container, not on a panel.** Where v5 called
  `Collapse.getOrCreateInstance(panel).show()`, v6 calls
  `Accordion.getOrCreateInstance(accordion).show(item)`, with the item given as
  an element or as its index. `hide()` and `toggle()` take the same argument,
  and `showAll()` / `hideAll()` open and close the whole accordion — the latter
  pair also handles the `name` juggling the HTML Standard requires, which is why
  expanding an exclusive accordion is a plugin call rather than a loop over
  `open`. None of it is needed to open and close items; the element does that
  on its own.
- **The header draws its focus ring on `:focus-visible`, not `:focus`.** A mouse
  click no longer paints a ring, keyboard focus still does, matching `.btn`.
- **New CSS variables:** `--cui-accordion-active-border-color` (the open item's
  border and the header's inset shadow, previously stuck on the resting border
  colour), plus `--cui-accordion-content-duration` and
  `--cui-accordion-content-easing`, which drive the panel transition and are
  read back by `accordion.js` so both paths animate for the same length of time.
- **The indicator can be an element instead of a background image.** Put an
  `<svg class="accordion-icon">` in the `<summary>` and the header stops drawing
  its own icon, so any icon set or `<img>` can be used without URL escaping an
  SVG into a custom property. `--cui-accordion-header-active-icon` selects the
  shape shown while the item is open, and a second element marked
  `.accordion-icon-active` does the same in markup — that is what a plus/minus
  accordion needs, where the icon is swapped rather than rotated. The default
  chevron and its rotation are unchanged when neither is used.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The open item is neutral, and `.theme-{color}` is what tints it.**
  `--cui-accordion-active-bg` and `--cui-accordion-active-color` were fixed to
  `--cui-primary-bg-subtle` / `--cui-primary-fg-emphasis`, so every accordion
  turned the brand colour on open whether or not that suited the page. They are
  `--cui-bg-2` and `--cui-fg-body` now, and the active tokens resolve
  through `--cui-theme-fg` / `-bg-subtle` / `-border` first — so the colour is a
  decision you make per accordion instead of one baked into the component. To
  keep the old look, put `.theme-primary` on the `.accordion`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`--cui-accordion-inner-border-radius` is gone; the header's corners follow
  `--cui-accordion-border-radius`.** The token was computed from the global
  `--cui-border-radius` when the stylesheet was built, so setting
  `--cui-accordion-border-radius` rounded the item while the header inside kept
  the page's default radius. The two corners are subtracted where they are
  used — `calc(var(--cui-accordion-border-radius) - var(--cui-accordion-border-width))`
  — so one token now shapes the whole item. Defaults render as before; drop any
  override of the removed variable.

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The header tokens are named after the header.** The summary is not a
  button any more, so `--cui-accordion-btn-*` is `--cui-accordion-header-*`
  (`-color`, `-bg`, `-icon`, `-active-icon`, `-icon-width`, `-icon-color`,
  `-active-icon-color`, `-icon-transform`, `-icon-transition`) and the
  `$accordion-header-*` Sass variables are `$accordion-header-*`. Rename in
  place; nothing else about them changed.
- **One padding token and one font size for the whole control.**
  `--cui-accordion-padding-x` / `-y` and `--cui-accordion-font-size` are read by
  the header and the body alike, so resizing an accordion is one declaration
  instead of four. `--cui-accordion-header-padding-*` (was `-btn-padding-*`)
  and `--cui-accordion-body-padding-*` are still there for resizing one part,
  declared on `.accordion` as `var(--cui-accordion-padding-*)`. Because they
  resolve where they are declared, override `--cui-accordion-padding-*` on
  `.accordion` (or a `.accordion-*` size class), not on a single
  `.accordion-item` — there it no longer reaches the header and the body.

#### Breadcrumb

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The breadcrumb has no bottom margin.** `.breadcrumb` sat on
  `margin-bottom: 1rem` through `$breadcrumb-margin-bottom` and
  `--cui-breadcrumb-margin-bottom`; both are gone and the list ends where its
  content ends, like every other navigation component. Add `.mb-5` (or set
  `margin-bottom` on the `<nav>`) where the page relied on that gap.
- **Three breadcrumb tokens have real defaults.** `--cui-breadcrumb-bg`,
  `--cui-breadcrumb-border-radius` and `--cui-breadcrumb-font-size` were `null`
  in Sass, so the first two were emitted empty (`--cui-breadcrumb-bg: ;`) and
  the third was not emitted at all. They are `transparent`,
  `var(--cui-radius-5)` and `inherit` now. Nothing renders differently on
  a default breadcrumb, and a font size set on an ancestor still reaches it —
  the one visible change is that a breadcrumb **given a background** now has
  rounded corners like every other surface. Set
  `--cui-breadcrumb-border-radius: 0` to square it off.
- **`--cui-breadcrumb-divider` is now declared on `.breadcrumb`, so set it
  there and not on a wrapper.** The property was read but never emitted, which
  left it out of DevTools and out of the CSS variables table, and meant a value
  set anywhere above the list reached the separators by inheritance. It is a
  real token now — and a token declared on `.breadcrumb` beats one inherited
  from an ancestor, so `<nav style="--cui-breadcrumb-divider: '>'">` stops
  working. Move it onto the `<ol class="breadcrumb">`.
- **A custom divider now survives in RTL.** `--cui-breadcrumb-divider-flipped`
  defaults to the main divider instead of to a second copy of the Sass default,
  so setting only `--cui-breadcrumb-divider` no longer leaves right-to-left
  pages on the built-in `/`. Set the flipped one only for a separator that
  points somewhere — `>` becomes `<` — as before.
- **New: `.breadcrumb-link`, an opt-in class that gives a step a padded,
  hoverable target.** Bare links keep rendering as they always did. On a
  `<button>` it looks identical to a link, which is what lets one step toggle a
  menu of collapsed items — the composition is documented under Collapsed
  items, together with the reason not to reach for `role="menu"` there.
- **New: `.breadcrumb-divider`, a separator you can put in the markup.** The
  automatic `::before` divider stays exactly as it was and existing markup is
  untouched, but a `<li class="breadcrumb-divider">` placed between two items
  carries whatever you put in it — an inline SVG, an icon, a character — with
  no URL escaping and no variable to set, and an empty one renders the default
  divider. The automatic divider steps aside wherever an element is placed
  (`.breadcrumb-item + .breadcrumb-item` stops matching), so the two never
  double up, and the spacing is identical either way. Give the element
  `aria-hidden="true"`, since a screen reader would otherwise count the
  separators as steps in the trail.

#### Avatar

- **The avatar carries its own colours, and `.theme-{color}` tints it.**
  `--cui-avatar-bg` and `--cui-avatar-color` are new: a bare avatar is a
  neutral circle instead of a transparent one, and a `.theme-*` class puts the
  theme colour behind the letters and the colour that reads on it in front of
  them. The docs used to reach for `bg-{color}` plus a hand-picked `text-*`,
  which fails WCAG AA on five of the six examples they shipped — measured 1.85
  on `bg-warning text-white`, 2.94 on info, 3.50 on success, 3.69 on danger,
  and 3.12 on `bg-secondary`, which carried no text colour at all. Every theme
  colour now lands between 4.56 and 18.03. Background utilities keep working on
  an avatar; they just no longer have to.
- **New: `.avatar-subtle`**, a tinted avatar that keeps the theme colour in the
  letters rather than behind them.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **One size token replaces the width/height pair.** `--cui-avatar-width` and
  `--cui-avatar-height` always carried the same value, in the base and in all
  four size classes, so setting a size meant writing it twice. They become
  `--cui-avatar-size`, and `--cui-avatar-status-width` / `-height` become
  `--cui-avatar-status-size` the same way.

  The label is derived from it — `calc(var(--cui-avatar-size) * .4)` — rather
  than listed per size. That ratio is what every size already used, so nothing
  renders differently; `--cui-avatar-font-size` still overrides it where a
  size needs its own.

  The Sass variables follow: `$avatar-width` / `$avatar-height` /
  `$avatar-font-size` / `$avatar-status-width` / `$avatar-status-height` become
  `$avatar-size` / `$avatar-status-size` plus `$avatar-font-size-ratio`, and
  `$avatar-sizes` keys drop to `size` and an optional `status-size`. The
  `$avatar-widths` map, deprecated since 5.1.0, is gone.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.avatar-img` fills the avatar and crops instead of stretching.** It was
  `height: auto`, which deformed every image that was not square; it is now
  `height: 100%` with `object-fit: cover`. Square images look the same.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The status indicator's ring follows the theme.** It was `1px solid $white`,
  a hardcoded white that read as a mistake in dark mode. It now comes from
  `--cui-avatar-status-border-color`, defaulting to the body background.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **A stacked avatar lifts on hover instead of pushing its neighbours.** The
  hover changed `margin-inline-end`, which moved every avatar after it; it now
  uses `transform`, so nothing else shifts. `$avatar-transition` changes from
  `margin .15s` to `transform .15s ease-in-out` accordingly.
- **Stacked avatars wear a ring and stop pulling what follows them.** Each
  avatar in a stack gets a border in the page background colour
  (`--cui-avatar-stack-ring-width` / `-color`), so overlapping photos stay
  separated; the last avatar drops its negative margin, which used to drag
  the content after the stack underneath it. The overlap is a token now —
  `--cui-avatar-stack-spacing`, the fraction of the avatar size — and
  `.avatar-stack-xs` through `-xl` size every avatar in a stack at once.
- **The status indicator follows `.theme-{color}` and is never invisible.**
  `.avatar-status` reads the theme slot and falls back to the new
  `--cui-avatar-status-bg` (a neutral grey), so a bare indicator reads as
  "offline" instead of rendering as an empty ring. Background utilities keep
  working on it.

#### Alert

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **An alert without a theme class carries its own surface.** `--cui-alert-bg`
  falls back to `var(--cui-bg-1)` instead of `transparent`, so a plain
  `.alert` no longer takes the color of whatever it sits on. Set
  `--cui-alert-bg: transparent` on the alert to restore the old rendering.

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The alert lays its children out in a row.** It is `display: flex` with
  `align-items: start` and `--cui-alert-gap` between children, so an icon next
  to a label needs no `d-flex` and no spacing utility. Text mixed with inline
  elements is a single anonymous item and is unaffected, but an alert built
  from **several block elements** — a heading, paragraphs, an `<hr>` — now sits
  side by side. Wrap that content in a `.vstack`:

  ```html
  <div class="alert alert-success" role="alert">
    <div class="vstack">
      <h4 class="alert-heading">Great job!</h4>
      <p>…</p>
    </div>
  </div>
  ```

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **A solid alert is `.alert-solid`, not a pile of utilities.** The docs used to
  say to drop the contextual class and reach for `bg-{color}` plus a `text-*`
  class picked by hand — and the examples hard-coded `text-white`, which fails
  WCAG AA on five of the eight theme colours: measured 3.50 on success, 3.69 on
  danger, 2.94 on info, 1.85 on warning and 1.10 on light, where white text on a
  near-white background is invisible. `.alert-solid` reads the theme's own
  contrast token, so every colour lands between 4.56 and 18.03 — including a
  theme colour you define yourself. Markup using the old recipe still renders;
  it is the contrast that was never right.

- **An alert marked `.fade show` fades in when it is added to the page.** The
  classes used to animate the alert in one direction only, because an element
  inserted with `.show` already on it has no earlier state to transition from;
  `@starting-style` supplies one. Nothing to change in your code — an alert you
  append at runtime animates without the class juggling that used to be needed,
  and an alert already in the markup is unaffected.

- **The close button is a flex item, not an absolutely positioned one.** It is
  pushed to the end with `margin-inline-start: auto`, so it no longer overlaps
  the text and the alert no longer reserves three paddings of room for it.
  A `.btn-close` that is a **direct child** of the alert still lands where it
  did, with or without `.alert-dismissible`, and `.alert-dismissible` itself is
  now an empty class kept for existing markup. One case does change: a close
  button nested inside a wrapper used to be positioned by the alert, and is now
  laid out by that wrapper like any other content. Move it up to be a direct
  child of the alert to keep it at the trailing edge.

- **An `<hr>` inside an alert takes the alert's border colour.** The alert
  redeclares `--cui-hr-border-color` from the theme's border colour, falling
  back to `--cui-alert-border-color`, so a separator in a `.theme-success`
  alert is green rather than the page's neutral grey, and one in an alert whose
  border you recoloured follows it. A variant such as `.alert-solid` paints its
  border with the surface colour, so there the separator takes the theme's
  border colour, or the text colour without one.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`--cui-alert-border` is gone.** The border is written in the rule from
  `--cui-alert-border-width` and the border colour, so that a `.theme-*` class
  can colour it. Set `--cui-alert-border-width` and `--cui-alert-border-color`
  instead, and `border-style` on the alert for a dashed or dotted line.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`$alert-dismissible-padding-r` is gone; the space for the close button
  follows `--cui-alert-padding-x`.** The variable was multiplied out in Sass
  when the stylesheet was built, so raising `--cui-alert-padding-x` widened
  three sides of a dismissible alert and left the fourth on the default: at
  `2rem` the alert had 32px of padding and 48px of room for the button, which
  also kept its own 16px padding. Both are subtracted from the tokens now —
  `calc(var(--cui-alert-padding-x) * 3)` and
  `calc(var(--cui-alert-padding-y) * 1.25) var(--cui-alert-padding-x)` — so one
  token resizes the whole alert. Defaults render as before; drop any override
  of the removed variable.

#### The close button is masked, not filtered

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

`.btn-close` used to paint a black glyph baked into its SVG and recolour it for
dark mode with a filter. The colour now comes from the element, the way the
check, radio, carousel and toggler icons already work:

```diff
- background: transparent var(--cui-btn-close-bg) center / 1em auto no-repeat;
- filter: var(--cui-btn-close-filter);
+ background-color: currentcolor;
+ mask-image: var(--cui-btn-close-icon);
```

- **`--cui-btn-close-filter` is removed**, together with `$btn-close-filter` and
  `$btn-close-filter-dark`. Recolour the button with `--cui-btn-close-color`
  instead of inverting the image.
- **`$btn-close-color` defaults to `inherit`**, so the glyph follows the surface
  it sits on. A close button inside a themed alert or a dark toast no longer
  needs a rule of its own to stay legible.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.btn-close-white` is gone**, deprecated since v5.0.0, and so is the
  dark-mode rule that forced the glyph white. Both restated what the inherited
  colour already produces on a surface that paints its own text — a
  `.text-bg-inverse` toast, a dark navbar's offcanvas, a `.bg-body.text-body`
  section under `data-coreui-theme="dark"`. Delete the class; the button
  renders identically without it. The rule additionally got the mirror case
  wrong: a `data-coreui-theme="light"` section inside a dark page inherited the
  forced white and drew a white icon on white.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`--cui-btn-close-width` and `-height` become `--cui-btn-close-icon-size`.**
  The glyph is square, so one variable sizes it; the padding keeps both axes
  (`--cui-btn-close-padding-y`, `-padding-x`). Nothing changes on screen: the
  glyph is still `1em` inside `.25em` of padding, so the pointer target stays
  24px at the default font size — the WCAG 2.2 minimum.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`--cui-btn-close-bg` is `--cui-btn-close-icon`** (`$btn-close-bg` →
  `$btn-close-icon`). It stopped being a background when the glyph moved to a
  mask; the name had been describing the old implementation.
- The SVG in `--cui-btn-close-icon` carries the shape only. A custom image works
  the same way — give it any colour, the mask ignores it.

#### Badge

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Colour comes from `.theme-*`, and `.badge-*` now names the variant.** A bare
  `.badge` used to be white text with no background of its own, so it was
  invisible until a `.text-bg-*` utility supplied both. It renders neutral on
  its own now — `--cui-badge-color` is `inherit` and `--cui-badge-bg` is
  `--cui-bg-2` — and a `.theme-*` class colours it:
  `<span class="badge theme-danger">`. The `.badge-*` namespace carries the
  three variants instead: solid (default), `.badge-subtle` and
  `.badge-outline`, each mapping the same theme roles.

  `.text-bg-*` still works and still sets both properties, so existing markup
  keeps rendering. Markup that paired `.badge` with a background utility alone
  (`<span class="badge bg-danger">`) now shows the inherited text colour on
  that background instead of white — swap it for `.theme-danger` or add a text
  colour utility.

- **The badge lays itself out.** `display` is `inline-flex` with
  `--cui-badge-gap` between children and `--cui-badge-min-height` as the floor,
  so an icon next to a label needs no spacing utility and badges keep one
  height across a table or a list.

- **The font size has a legibility floor.** `--cui-badge-font-size` is
  `clamp(12px, .75em, .75em)`, so a badge inside small text stops shrinking at
  12px while still scaling up with a larger parent.
- **New: `.badge-position-{top|bottom}-{start|end}`.** One class pins a badge to
  a corner of its positioned parent, centred on the corner, and flips with the
  writing direction. It replaces the `position-absolute top-0 start-100
  translate-middle` utility stack the docs used to recommend; that stack still
  works. The `.btn .badge` nudge (`position: relative; top: -1px`) is written as
  `.btn :where(.badge)` so a position class on a badge inside a button wins;
  a stylesheet that relied on beating `.btn .badge` with two classes now beats
  it with one.

- **New: `--cui-badge-border-width` / `--cui-badge-border-color` (transparent),
  `.badge-lg`, and a link reset.** The border tokens are what `.badge-outline`
  switches, `.badge-lg` joins `.badge-sm` on the size scale, and `a.badge` no
  longer inherits the underline from the reboot.

#### Carousel slides by scrolling

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The engine is native CSS scroll snapping, not transforms.** The track
  (`.carousel-inner`) is a scroll container: dragging, swiping and momentum
  come from the browser, and programmatic jumps animate the scroll position
  over a fixed 300ms. The transform choreography is gone with its state
  classes — `.carousel-item-next`, `.carousel-item-prev`,
  `.carousel-item-start`, `.carousel-item-end` and `.pointer-event` no longer
  exist; react to the `slide`/`slid` events instead of styling them. The
  `.slide` class is inert — leave it in old markup or drop it, nothing reads
  it any more. The markup skeleton (indicators + inner with items +
  controls) is unchanged, and the controls, indicators and captions keep
  their overlaid layout.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`ride` is `autoplay`, `wrap` is `ends`, and `touch` is gone.**
  `data-coreui-autoplay="true"` (boolean — the `"carousel"` spelling is gone)
  cycles from page load; `ends` takes `"loop"` (default — continues
  seamlessly where the layout allows), `"wrap"` (the old jump) or `"stop"`
  (hard ends with the matching control disabled). Touch needs no option:
  the browser owns it. Autoplay now turns off for good once the user
  interacts (WCAG 2.2.2), and while it runs the active indicator fills like
  a progress bar over the slide's interval.
- **New layout knobs and a pause control.** `--cui-carousel-items`,
  `--cui-carousel-items-gap` and `--cui-carousel-items-peek` put several
  slides in the viewport; `.carousel-center` snaps the active slide to the
  middle and `.carousel-auto` lets slides keep their own width. A button
  with `.carousel-control-play-pause` (holding `.carousel-control-pause-icon`
  and `.carousel-control-play-icon`) toggles autoplay and swaps its
  `aria-label` between `data-coreui-pause-label` and
  `data-coreui-play-label`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.carousel-dark`, the `carousel-dark()` mixin and `$carousel-dark-*` are
  gone.** The chrome colours are `light-dark()` pairs resolved on the
  carousel itself, so the standard `data-coreui-theme="dark"` attribute is
  the dark variant — and `data-coreui-theme="light"` pins the white chrome
  on a dark page the same way.
- **Every chrome measurement is a `--cui-carousel-*` token** declared on
  `.carousel` — control width and opacities, icon images and sizes,
  indicator geometry and transitions, caption metrics, the play/pause
  button — so the whole carousel restyles at runtime without recompiling.
- **Crossfade timing moved to tokens.** `$carousel-transition-duration` and
  `$carousel-transition` are gone; the fade reads
  `--cui-carousel-fade-duration` and `--cui-carousel-fade-timing`, and the
  slides stack on a grid instead of floating.

#### Autocomplete and Multi Select share one options menu

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The generated options dropdown now uses unified `combobox-*` classes** in
  both components: `combobox-popup`, `combobox-options`, `combobox-option`
  (+`-with-checkbox`), `combobox-optgroup(-label)`, `combobox-options-empty`,
  `combobox-header`, `combobox-all`, with the state classes
  `selected` and `indeterminate`. The former `autocomplete-dropdown`/
  `autocomplete-option(s)`/`autocomplete-optgroup*` and
  `form-multi-select-dropdown*`/`-options*`/`-option*`/`-optgroup*`/`-all*`/
  `form-multi-selected`/`form-multi-select-indeterminate` classes are gone,
  along with their Sass variables and CSS custom properties — theme the
  shared surface through `$combobox-*` / `--cui-combobox-*` instead. This
  markup is generated by the plugins (authors don't write it), so only custom
  CSS targeting the dropdown internals needs updating. The field/selection
  markup of both components is unchanged.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The options list is now a [List box](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/list-box/) instance, so
  the `combobox-*` option classes are gone.** `.combobox-popup` stays as the
  panel; everything inside it is List box markup:

  | v6 alpha | now |
  | --- | --- |
  | `.combobox-header` | `.list-box-header` |
  | `.combobox-all`, `.combobox-all-with-checkbox` | `.list-box-select-all` |
  | `.combobox-options` | `.list-box-options` |
  | `.combobox-option`, `.combobox-option-with-checkbox` | `.list-box-option` |
  | `.combobox-option-indicator` | `.list-box-option-indicator` |
  | `.combobox-optgroup` | `.list-box-section` |
  | `.combobox-optgroup-label`, `.combobox-optgroup-label-with-checkbox` | `.list-box-section-label` |
  | `.combobox-options-empty` | `.list-box-empty` |
  | `data-value` on an option | `data-coreui-value` |

  The same move takes the tokens: `--cui-combobox-header-*`,
  `--cui-combobox-select-all-*`, `--cui-combobox-options-*`,
  `--cui-combobox-option-*` and `--cui-combobox-optgroup-*` are replaced by
  `--cui-list-box-header-*`, `--cui-list-box-padding-*` / `-gap`,
  `--cui-list-box-option-*` and `--cui-list-box-section-label-*`. Only
  `--cui-combobox-popup-min-width` is left on `.combobox-popup`. Option
  padding follows the List box (`.5rem .75rem`, indicator in its own grid
  column) rather than the wider `1.25rem` the combobox surface used.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Multi Select's `selectAllStyle` and `optionsGroupsStyle` are removed.**
  A list has one marker, and `indicator` decides it for the options, the
  selectable group labels and the select all button alike. Replace
  `selectAllStyle: 'text'` / `optionsGroupsStyle: 'text'` with
  `indicator: 'none'` when you want the whole list without checkboxes.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Autocomplete keeps the focus in its input while the options list is
  open.** Options take no `tabindex` and are never focused; the input carries
  `aria-activedescendant`, and the arrows, <kbd>Enter</kbd> and
  <kbd>Space</kbd> are handled there, so <kbd>Home</kbd>, <kbd>End</kbd> and
  the space bar keep working as text keys.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Multi Select opens a dialog instead.** The panel is
  `role="dialog"` named by the control, and the focus moves into it: onto the
  search box when `search` is on, otherwise onto the option the list points
  at. Options take a roving `tabindex` and the arrows move the focus itself,
  so there is no `aria-activedescendant` to read any more.
  <kbd>Tab</kbd> cycles through the panel and the search box without leaving
  them, and <kbd>Escape</kbd> closes the panel and hands the focus back to the
  control. The select all button left the arrow order — it sits in the panel
  header, reachable with <kbd>Tab</kbd>, and carries `aria-pressed`
  (`true`/`false`/`mixed`); selectable group labels are in the arrow order in
  its place.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Multi Select's `search` takes `true`, `false` or `'external'`, and `search: 'global'` is gone.**
  Typing reaches the search box from anywhere in the component by default,
  which is what `'global'` used to turn on, so `data-coreui-search="global"`
  becomes `data-coreui-search="true"`. Set `globalSearch: false` to keep
  typing local: the search box then filters only while it has the focus, and
  a keystroke over the options list jumps to the first option starting with
  it. Any other string throws a `TypeError`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Multi Select's `value` wins over options marked `selected`.** It used to
  add to them, in the markup and in `options`, and later options applied the
  start value again; now a given `value` is the
  selection, also for options that arrive later for the first time, as in the
  React and Vue editions, while options shown before keep their state. An array of numbers matches the options, and `value: ''` reads
  like an empty `data-coreui-value`.
- **Multi Select takes `search: 'external'`.** The search box then filters
  nothing, `search.coreui.multi-select` carries the `query`, and
  `setOptions()` replaces the options without rebuilding the control or
  touching the selection; a selected value the new options lack keeps its
  chip and is still sent with the form, unless it is disabled. `setLoading()` shows a
  `loadingLabel` row below the options meanwhile, and they stay usable. `resetSelectionOnOptionsChange` empties the selection when the new
  options carry different values, for options that depend on another field.
- **Multi Select's `search.coreui.multi-select` follows the text, not the
  keys.** It fires when the text of the search box changes, a paste included,
  and no longer on a key that leaves the text alone, such as an arrow, or when
  the panel opens or closes over an empty box.
- **Multi Select's `dispose()` gives the native select back the options it had
  before the component started.** A select built from `options` in the config,
  or one whose options `setOptions()` replaced, used to keep
  the generated options; an empty `<select>` given `options` is empty again
  after `dispose()`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Multi Select sends one `change.coreui.multi-select` per change of the
  selection.** The clear button, `deselectAll()`, `selectAll()` and a pick
  that replaces the value of a single select used to send one event for every
  option they touched; they send one now, once the native select holds the new
  value and the control shows it, the way the native `change` works. An action
  that changes nothing sends no event, native ones included, and the component
  no longer sends one for each preselected option while it builds or rebuilds.
  A selection the page gives sends none, from `setValue()` or from a given
  `value` that options from `setOptions()` bring later, and a native reset of
  the form that changes the selection sends one, as Autocomplete, Chip Input
  and Combobox do.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Form controls fire their change event once per change of the value, on a
  form reset as well, and never from a value the page gives.** OTP Input's
  `change.coreui.otp-input`, Rating's
  `reset()`, Autocomplete's `clear()` and Range's `changed.coreui.range` used
  to fire even when the value stayed; they fire only when it changes now, and
  Range no longer fires while it starts or on the `change` after an `input`.
  OTP Input and Range Slider now fire on a native form reset that changes the
  value, and Date Input sends the native `input` and `change` of its value
  field there too. `setValue()` of OTP Input does not fire. A form
  reset brings Rating and Range Slider back to the value they were created
  with, not one a later `setValue()` gave, and OTP Input's `reset()` puts back
  the code it started with, as a native reset does. A stepper keeps the
  validation marks of a form reset a listener cancels.
- **Multi Select keeps its selection through `setOptions()`**; it used to fall
  back to the options marked `selected`. A selected option that is disabled, also inside a
  disabled group, shows as a disabled chip and stays through the clear button,
  `deselectAll()` and <kbd>Backspace</kbd>; the options of a disabled group
  are disabled in the list and in the native select as well, so, like any
  disabled option, they are not sent with the form. A single select whose
  selection is empty holds its `value=""` placeholder option, or none, instead
  of letting the browser fall back to the first option, so the form gets no
  option the control does not show.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Autocomplete's `search` takes only `'external'`, and global typing is
  `globalSearch`, on by default.** The array and the comma list are gone, so
  `search: ['external', 'global']` and `data-coreui-search="external, global"`
  become `search: 'external'`, and `search: 'global'` goes away: typing on the
  picker or clear button, or in the panel, reaches the field without it. Set
  `globalSearch: false` to keep a keystroke where the focus is. An array now
  fails the option type check, so `new coreui.Autocomplete(el, { search:
  ['external'] })` throws until it is updated, and a string other than
  `'external'` no longer turns external search on, so a page still writing
  the comma list in a data attribute filters the server's results again. The
  space bar and shortcuts with Ctrl or Cmd stay with the button that has the
  focus.
- Autocomplete no longer gives the host `tabindex="-1"` and keeps a
  `tabindex` the page wrote out of the tab order while it lives, so the field
  is the only tab stop; a click on the frame puts the focus in the field
  rather than only opening the panel. With
  `search: 'external'` the panel opens for the options you set even when they
  do not contain the typed text.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Autocomplete's typeahead hint no longer reaches the server.** With
  `showHints` on, the hint input behind the field carried a `name` and, being
  readonly rather than disabled, was serialized with the form: the configured
  `name` when there was one, `<uid>-hint` when there was not. A form therefore
  submitted either the field twice with an empty string in front of the value,
  or an extra `…-hint` key nobody asked for. The hint now carries no `name`, so
  `new FormData(form).getAll(name)` returns one entry and `form.elements[name]`
  is an input rather than a `RadioNodeList`. Code written around the old shape,
  such as reading `getAll(name)[1]`, needs the plain value.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Multi Select's search field no longer reaches the server.** The input that
  filters the options carried a `name` and is a plain text input, so every form
  holding a Multi Select submitted whatever the user had typed to narrow the
  list: `search-<name>` when the select had a name, `search-<uid>` when it did
  not. It carries no `name` now, the `search-…` key is gone from the payload,
  and the input sets `autocomplete="off"` so the browser stops offering to fill
  it.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **An unnamed Multi Select stays out of the form.** A `<select>` the page had
  not named was given `name="<uid>"`, so its value was submitted under a key
  nobody chose. The attribute is written only when there is a name, from the
  option or from the element. `dispose()` also takes back the `id` and the
  `name` the component added, where it used to leave both behind.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **An unnamed Autocomplete stays out of the form too.** Its input took a
  generated `name` when the option was not set, so a form carried a key nobody
  chose. It does not now. The generated `id` is unaffected.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Validation state comes from `validationState`.** `invalid` and `valid` are
  deprecated aliases of `validationState: 'invalid'` and `'valid'`, and
  `validationState` also takes a state from `$form-validation-states`. A given
  `'invalid'`, from the options or from an `.is-invalid` class in the markup, now
  blocks the submit, and the next change the user makes to the value drops it,
  while a change from code keeps it. The markup class is read once, when the
  component starts, so it no longer comes back when the text returns to the
  value it started with, a native form reset drops it, and a class written later
  is not read. A validation that reports the input, from a
  submit or the page's own `checkValidity()`, now shows on the frame, and the
  autocomplete is the only one that writes `aria-invalid` and the message link on
  its input: the form plugin and the stepper leave the input to it. See
  [Validation](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/autocomplete/#validation).

#### Menu (new) and Dropdown

- **New component: Menu** — contextual overlays with submenus, container
  teleport, responsive placement and logical (`start`/`end`) placements,
  ported from Bootstrap v6 with CoreUI's class conventions. See the
  [Menu docs](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/menu/).
- **New component: [Context Menu](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/context-menu/)** (PRO) — a
  right-click, long-press or <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F10</kbd> inside an element
  with `data-coreui-toggle="context-menu"` opens a `.menu` at the pointer and
  suppresses the browser's own menu. Built on Menu, so items, submenus,
  keyboard navigation and dismissal are the same; `show({ x, y })` opens it at
  a point from JavaScript.
- **Dropdown is now a compatibility layer over Menu.** The v5 markup, classes
  and `*.coreui.dropdown` events keep working unchanged — the plugin shares
  Menu's engine underneath. Where their options overlap, Dropdown also
  understands Menu's additions (for example `container`). **Deprecation
  notice: Dropdown may be removed in v7** — prefer Menu for new work.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The responsive alignment classes `.dropdown-menu{-sm|-md|-lg|-xl|-2xl}{-start|-end}`
  moved to `coreui-legacy.css`, and `.dropup`/`.dropend`/`.dropstart` no longer
  carry the menu's static position.** Alignment that changes with the viewport
  is a responsive placement on the toggle — `data-coreui-placement="bottom-start
  lg:bottom-end"` — which now also works with `data-coreui-display="static"` and
  inside a navbar, because the plugin writes the resolved placement onto the
  menu as `data-coreui-placement` and the stylesheet positions the static menu
  from that attribute rather than from the wrapper class. `dropdown.css`
  loses every `@media`; the direction classes keep the caret and still decide
  the placement when no option is given. `.dropdown-menu-end` and
  `.dropdown-menu-start` stay.

  ```diff
  - <ul class="dropdown-menu dropdown-menu-lg-end">
  + <button data-coreui-toggle="dropdown" data-coreui-placement="bottom-start lg:bottom-end">
  + <ul class="dropdown-menu">
  ```

#### List Box (new)

- **New component: [List Box](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/list-box/)** (PRO) — a keyboard-driven
  list of values on the ARIA listbox pattern, with `none`/`single`/`multiple`
  selection, sections, descriptions, links, an empty state and an
  `activeDescendant` mode that leaves the focus in your own input.

#### Combobox (new)

- **New component: [Combobox](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/combobox/)** — a `.form-control` toggle next
  to a popup [List box](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/list-box/), where the options are markup you
  write, with single and multiple selection, an optional search, a checkbox
  indicator and a hidden select that takes part in form validation and
  submission.

#### Transfer (new)

- **New component: [Transfer](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/transfer/)** (PRO) — two list boxes and
  a pair of buttons that move the selected options between them, with a
  select-all header and counter per list, an optional search, a one-way mode and
  an announcement of every move through `announce()`.

#### Form (new)

- **New plugin: [Form](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/validation/#usage)** (PRO) — validates a form with
  `data-coreui-validate` and `novalidate` on submit: stops an invalid submit,
  marks the invalid controls with `.is-invalid` and `aria-invalid`, links their
  messages with `aria-describedby` and moves focus to the first. It replaces
  the starter script of the validation page, see
  [Validation](#validation-data-coreui-validate-replaces-was-validated).

#### Modal and Offcanvas on the native `<dialog>`

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Both components render on the native `<dialog>` element now**, opened with
  `showModal()`/`show()` and closed with `close()`. The browser provides the
  top layer (no `z-index` management), the `::backdrop`, focus trapping,
  background inertness and the dialog ARIA semantics — the markup drops
  `role`, `aria-modal`, `aria-hidden` and `tabindex="-1"`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The modal wrapper layers are gone.** `.modal > .modal-dialog >
  .modal-content` flattens to a single `<dialog class="modal">` holding
  `.modal-header`/`.modal-body`/`.modal-footer` directly. Size
  (`.modal-sm/-lg/-xl`) and fullscreen (`.modal-fullscreen*`) modifiers sit on
  the dialog itself. `.modal-dialog-scrollable` is renamed to
  `.modal-scrollable`; `.modal-dialog-centered` is gone — the browser centers
  a modal dialog by default. Offcanvas markup only swaps the tag:
  `<dialog class="offcanvas offcanvas-start">`.
- **Legacy v5 markup keeps working — for now.** When a component is
  instantiated on v5 markup (via data API or JavaScript), the plugin rebuilds
  it into the `<dialog>` structure at runtime and logs a console warning. The
  old element stays in the DOM as an inert `display: contents` shell around
  the new dialog, so existing element references and event listeners keep
  working (our events bubble through it). Selectors and custom CSS targeting
  `.modal-dialog`/`.modal-content` are the one thing that cannot be preserved.
  **This transitional layer will be removed in v7.**
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.modal-backdrop` and `.offcanvas-backdrop` are gone** — the backdrop is
  the native `::backdrop` pseudo-element. Style it through the new
  `--cui-modal-backdrop-bg`/`--cui-modal-backdrop-opacity` and
  `--cui-offcanvas-backdrop-*` tokens. `$zindex-modal-backdrop` and
  `$zindex-offcanvas-backdrop` are removed.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Animation opt-in is inverted.** Modals animate by default; `.fade` is gone
  and `.modal-instant` / `.offcanvas-instant` disable animation instead. The
  auto-upgrade maps a missing `.fade` to `.modal-instant`. The offcanvas
  `.showing` state class is replaced by the native `[open]` attribute
  (`.hiding` remains for the exit transition).
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The Modal `focus` option is removed** — the native dialog always traps
  focus. `handleUpdate()` remains as a no-op for API compatibility; the
  scrollbar-compensation logic it triggered no longer exists (scroll lock is
  `overflow: hidden` + `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on the root element, with
  the `dialog-open` class).
- **New capabilities:** a `modal: false` option opens the dialog non-modally
  (page stays interactive); a `cancel.coreui.modal` event fires when a modal
  is dismissed with Escape; toggling from a trigger inside an open modal swaps
  to the target modal with a seamless backdrop (no flash); modals can be
  stacked — the top layer manages paint order.
- **Sass:** `$modal-transition` and `$modal-show-transform` are replaced by
  `$modal-transition-duration`/`$modal-transition-timing`; the offcanvas
  transition token splits the same way
  (`--cui-offcanvas-transition-duration`/`-timing`).

#### Dialog (new) and Drawer (new)

- **One backdrop model for both.** `:root` emits `--cui-backdrop-color`,
  `--cui-backdrop-opacity` and `--cui-backdrop-blur`; the dialog's and the
  drawer's `::backdrop` mix colour and opacity from them, so either can be
  retuned alone. The looks are unchanged (dialog: black at 50%/65%, drawer:
  the page colour at 25%), and `.backdrop-scrim`, `.backdrop-translucent` and
  `.backdrop-none` swap them from the markup. `.dialog-no-backdrop` and
  `.drawer-no-backdrop` keep working. The sidebar's backdrop tokens are
  `--cui-sidebar-backdrop-zindex/-bg/-opacity` now (were unprefixed
  `--cui-backdrop-*`, which the new root tokens would have shadowed).

- **New components: Dialog and Drawer** — the native-`<dialog>` popup and
  side panel with the Bootstrap 6 look and `dialog-*` / `drawer-*` classes:
  Dialog is a centered floating panel over a blurred backdrop with optional
  slide entry variants; Drawer is a floating, inset, rounded panel with
  translucent/sheet/fullscreen/fit-content variants and responsive classes
  (`.drawer-sm`…`.drawer-2xl`). Both share the DialogBase behavior with
  Modal/Offcanvas, which keep the classic look and the v5 vocabulary as
  **compatibility layers over it — deprecated in v6 and removable in v7**, so
  reach for Dialog and Drawer in new work. See the
  [Dialog docs](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/dialog/) and [Drawer docs](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/drawer/).

#### Translucent surfaces share two tokens

`.navbar-translucent`, `.toast-translucent`, `.card-translucent`,
`.drawer-translucent` and `.menu-translucent` used to carry their own mix
amount and filter — two as Sass variables, three as literals — at 85% or 80%
depending on the component. All five now read `--cui-translucent-opacity`
(80%) and `--cui-translucent-backdrop-filter` (`blur(5px) saturate(180%)`)
from `:root`, so one override retunes every translucent surface and one on an
element retunes that surface alone. Consequences: the navbar, the toast and
the card become slightly more see-through (85% → 80%; set
`--cui-translucent-opacity: 85%` on them to keep the old look), and
`$navbar-translucent-bg-opacity`, `$navbar-translucent-backdrop-filter`,
`$toast-translucent-bg-opacity`, `$toast-translucent-backdrop-filter`,
`$card-translucent-bg-opacity` and `$card-translucent-backdrop-filter` are
