removed in favour of `$translucent-opacity` and
`$translucent-backdrop-filter`. The card's caps keep their own amount as the
`--cui-card-translucent-cap-bg-opacity` token on `.card-translucent`.
#### Calendar

- **Changed.** **The range band sits under the day cells and follows the
  theme.** `--cui-calendar-cell-range-bg` is `--cui-primary-bg-subtle` (the
  theme's `bg-subtle` under a `.theme-*` class) instead of a 12.5% tint laid
  over the cells, so the selected start and end keep their solid fill and the
  day numbers are no longer tinted. A hovered day in the range takes
  `--cui-primary-bg-muted` (the theme's `bg-muted`), one step stronger than the
  band.
- **Changed.** **`minDate` and `maxDate` are compared by the day.** v5 compared
  a day cell at midnight with the instant, so a limit that carried a time of
  day, such as `minDate: new Date()`, disabled its own day. Now that day can be
  picked; the Date Picker's time selects and the field bound the time on it.
- **Changed.** **A week row stands for the ISO week that holds most of its
  days.** With `firstDayOfWeek` other than `1` a row spans two ISO weeks, and
  v5 numbered it by its last day but picked the week of its first day: a
  Sunday-first row from August 30 to September 5, 2026 showed 36 and picked
  `2026W35`, and that value given back as `startDate` did not select the
  same row. The number, the picked value, the weeks of `startDate`, `endDate`,
  `minDate` and `maxDate`, and the range and its preview now all follow the
  row's majority week, so that row is `2026W36` and the picked date is its
  Monday, August 31, the date `2026W36` stands for. With Tuesday to Thursday
  first, the numbers move instead, so the row from September 1 to 7 shows 36
  where it showed 37.
- **Changed.** **In week selection `disabledDates` disables a week only when
  it disables every day of it**, as it does for a month, quarter or year.
  Before, a week was disabled when its row's first day was: with Monday first
  `disabledDates: [new Date(2026, 7, 31)]` disabled week 36, and with Sunday
  first a function that disables weekends disabled every row. A range of
  weeks across a week that holds a disabled day is kept, as a range of months
  is.
- **Changed.** **In week selection `minDate` and `maxDate` count as whole
  weeks**, so their weeks can be picked on any first day of the week, as the
  month, quarter or year of a limit can in those selections, and the months
  view opens a month only when its page holds a week inside them. Before, a
  `minDate` later than the first day of its row disabled that row, and with
  Tuesday to Thursday first a `maxDate` of `2026W36` disabled the row of week
  36.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`cellHover` is gone.** It reported the date under the pointer or the focus
  for the hover preview in the date picker's field, which v6 no longer draws.
  To follow the pointer or the focus over a calendar, listen for `mouseover`
  or `focusin` on its element: each day, month, quarter and year cell the
  calendar shows carries its date in `data-coreui-date`, in the
  `toDateString()` form, and a week row carries it on the day cells it shows.
  Unlike the event, the listeners also see cells that cannot be picked, and
  only `mouseout` or `focusout` tells that the pointer or the focus left.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`selectAdjacementDays` and `showAdjacementDays` are spelled `selectAdjacentDays`
  and `showAdjacentDays`** — the option keys, the `data-coreui-select-adjacent-days`
  / `data-coreui-show-adjacent-days` attributes and the picker passthrough alike. The
  typo is fixed in the React and Vue libraries in the same release.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`update(config)` is gone; the dates change through `setStartDate()`,
  `setEndDate()` and `setCalendarDate()`**, and the other options are fixed once
  the calendar starts. A call like `update({ startDate })` used to reset every
  other option (`range`, `calendars`, `locale`, …). See
  [Live changes go through setters, not `update(config)`](#live-changes-go-through-setters-not-updateconfig).
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Each grid is one tab stop, and `tabindex` no longer marks a selectable cell.**
  The arrows, `Home`, `End` and the page keys move within a calendar and Tab
  leaves it, which is what `role="grid"` promises. Every cell inside `minDate` /
  `maxDate` can take the focus, also one that cannot be picked: the keys stop
  on a date in `disabledDates` instead of skipping it, a screen reader
  announces it as unavailable, and Enter and Space do nothing there, so a
  month where nothing can be picked is explored like any other. A selectable
  day, month, quarter, year or week row carries `data-coreui-selectable`, and
  `tabindex="0"` sits on one cell per grid, so a two-month range picker keeps a
  stop in each month that reaches into the limits. Custom CSS or scripts that reached for
  `.calendar-cell[tabindex="0"]` need `.calendar-cell[data-coreui-selectable]`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Day cells write `data-coreui-date` in the `toDateString()` form**
  (`"Thu Sep 17 2026"`), like the month, quarter and year cells, instead of the
  full `Date#toString()` value. A script that selected a day cell with
  `[data-coreui-date="${date}"]` needs `[data-coreui-date="${date.toDateString()}"]`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Picking a date no longer fires `calendarDateChange` and keeps the month on
  screen.** A picked day of an adjacent month (`selectAdjacentDays`) used to
  become the calendar date while the grid stayed put, so the next-month button
  then skipped a month; it now stays where it was and shows as selected with an
  outline on a subtle background instead of the solid fill
  (`--cui-calendar-cell-selected-adjacent-color`,
  `--cui-calendar-cell-selected-adjacent-bg`,
  `--cui-calendar-cell-selected-adjacent-hover-color`,
  `--cui-calendar-cell-selected-adjacent-hover-bg`).
  Listen to `startDateChange` / `endDateChange` for the picked date.
- A selected day or week keeps its colour under the pointer, a shade darker
  (`--cui-calendar-cell-selected-hover-bg`,
  `--cui-calendar-cell-selected-hover-color`); it used to take the hover colour
  until the pointer left.
- In the years view the navigation names the page it shows, `2020 – 2031`,
  instead of the one year the page is built around, so turning a page is
  announced as the new range. The grid label and the navigation write the range
  in the locale's own digits.
- In the years view the double arrows and `Page Down` / `Page Up` turn the
  calendar by the twelve years a page shows instead of ten, so a turn no longer
  repeats two years of the previous page.
- In the years view the year button goes back to the view that picks the date
  instead of doing nothing, and it is disabled in year selection, where the
  years view is the one that picks.
- Without `weekNumbersLabel` the week-number column is named for screen readers
  with the locale's word for week, in a visually hidden span; before, its
  header was empty. Each week number is named the same way with its number
  (`Week 36`), and the new `ariaWeekNumberLabel` option takes a function of
  the week number to name it yourself.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The years view names its double arrows with new `ariaNavNextYearsLabel` /
  `ariaNavPrevYearsLabel` options** (`'Next 12 years'` / `'Previous 12 years'`)
  instead of the year labels, which stay on the days, months and quarters
  views. A page that translates `ariaNavNextYearLabel` / `ariaNavPrevYearLabel`
  needs to translate these two as well.
- **A grid in which nothing can be picked is described by the new
  `ariaNothingToPickLabel` option** (`'Nothing on this page can be picked'`),
  also announced when the calendar turns to such a page, so a page that
  translates the other calendar labels translates this one too.
- **A pick is announced through the new `ariaSelectedDateAnnouncement` and
  `ariaSelectedRangeAnnouncement` options** (`'Selected date: {date}'`,
  `'Selected range: {start} to {end}'`); before, picking a date with
  <kbd>Enter</kbd> left a screen reader silent. A page that translates the other
  calendar labels translates these two as well.
- The toggle of a date picker and of a date range picker is named with the
  value the picker holds (`Toggle calendar, Friday, August 14, 2026`), so a
  screen reader reads the picked date when the popup closes and focus goes back
  to it. A toggle the page named itself keeps its name.
- The month and year between the navigation arrows are no longer a live region.
  A page turn is announced through `announce()`, inside the panel of a date
  picker when the calendar sits in one, and a key that leaves a day or a week
  row names no page, since the date that takes focus names it: a key that moved
  from August 31 to September 1 used to be read twice, as the date and then as
  `September 2026`.
- A locale whose own calendar has different months (Persian by default in
  `fa-IR`, or an Islamic, Hebrew or Chinese calendar asked for with `-u-ca-`)
  now shows the calendar in the Gregorian calendar, in its own language and
  digits. The grid was always Gregorian, so `fa-IR` used to show September 1,
  2026 as the 10th of Shahrivar 1405 and name the January cell Dey. Calendars
  that share the Gregorian months, such as Thai Buddhist or Japanese, keep
  their year numbering in the calendar. The date field lays out and reads
  Gregorian numbers for every locale, without the locale's era.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **A cell drawn by `renderDayCell` no longer gets an `aria-label`**, so its
  content is what a screen reader reads. Every render function receives the
  cell's full name as `meta.label` (`Wednesday, August 12, 2026`,
  `August 2026`, `Q3 2026`, `2026`); put it in the cell, for example in a
  `visually-hidden` span next to a short visible label marked
  `aria-hidden="true"`. Day cells the calendar draws itself announce the full
  date with the weekday instead of the short numeric date (`8/12/2026`), and
  in week selection the row that takes focus is named by the days it spans
  (`August 10 – 16, 2026`), after its week number when week numbers are shown
  (`Week 33, August 10 – 16, 2026`), instead of reading out all seven.
- The copy of today among an adjacent month's days no longer takes the
  `today` class, `aria-current="date"` or `meta.isToday`, so a two-month
  calendar marks one current date and a date picker opens on today only when
  its own month is shown. `renderDayCell` receives the full `meta` in week
  selection too, not only `label`.
- A cell that cannot be picked carries `aria-disabled="true"`: a day outside
  `minDate` / `maxDate` or in `disabledDates`, a week row, month, quarter or
  year outside the range, and in day selection a day among an adjacent
  month's days unless `selectAdjacentDays` is on. Such an adjacent day is no
  longer `aria-selected` either, and `renderDayCell` gets `isDisabled: true`
  and `isSelected: false` for it, so the copy of a picked date that fills the
  next panel reads as unavailable filler.
- With `selectAdjacentDays`, the range and its hover preview are drawn on the
  adjacent month's days as well, and `renderDayCell` gets `isInRange: true`
  for them, as for the month's own days. Before, both stopped at the edge of
  the month.
- With `range`, the grid of the view that picks the range is
  `aria-multiselectable`, and every day, week row, month, quarter or year of
  the selected range that can be picked carries `aria-selected="true"`, not
  only its two ends, so a screen reader announces the ones in between as
  selected. The months and years views of a day range only navigate and keep
  it on the ends, and `meta.isSelected` still marks only the ends.
- In week selection, `renderDayCell` gets the `isDisabled`, `isInRange` and
  `isSelected` of the day's week row, the way the calendar draws it: every day
  of a selected week is `isSelected`, every day of a week in the range is
  `isInRange`, and a day is `isDisabled` only when its row cannot be picked.
  Before, each field described the day alone, so a selected week reported
  only the day holding its start or end date as selected.
- A year below 100 stays as written: a calendar on December of year 99 draws
  the days of year 99, where it drew 1999, and its next panel shows January of
  year 100, where it showed January 2000. The month, quarter and year views,
  the arrow keys and paging keep the year the same way. Years before 1 cannot
  be picked, as dates before `minDate` cannot: their cells are disabled, and
  the arrow keys and paging stop at 1 January of year 1.
- A year string written in four digits keeps its year: `0026-05`, `0026W05`,
  `0026Q2`, `0026` and `12/31/0099` given to the calendar stand for the years
  26 and 99, where they became 2026 and 1999. Two digits still expand to the
  century within 50 years of today. The value reported for week, month,
  quarter and year selection writes the year in at least four digits
  (`0026-05`, where it was `26-05`), so it reads back as the same year.
- **Additive:** `startDateChange` and `endDateChange` now carry a `dateObject`
  property with the underlying `Date`, alongside the existing `date` property.
  `date` remains formatted per `selectionType` — a `Date` for day selection, but
  a string for week (`"2026W27"`), month (`"2026-01"`), quarter (`"2026Q1"`) and
  year (`"2026"`). Existing listeners are unaffected.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`startDateChange` and `endDateChange` no longer report a date that stays
  empty.** Picking a day before the start of a range, or an end across a
  disabled day, used to fire `endDateChange` with `null` although no end had
  been picked, and clearing a range across a disabled day before a start was
  picked fired `startDateChange` with `null`. A day picked again is still
  reported.
- `.months` and `.years` are gone from the stylesheet. Their one rule hid a
  `.time-picker` that followed a `.calendar` panel in the months or years view
  among its siblings, where no picker renders one, so it matched nothing. The calendar names
  the view it shows on the cell content: `.calendar-cell-inner.day`, `.month`,
  `.quarter` and `.year`.
- The navigation puts the year button before the month button in locales that
  write the year first (for example Japanese, Chinese, Korean, Hungarian or
  Lithuanian), the way the locale's own dates read.

#### Date Picker / Date Range Picker

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

**The pickers are rewritten.** `DatePicker`, `DateRangePicker` and `TimePicker`
are now a composition of the section-based input fields
([date input](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/date-input/)), the [calendar](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/components/calendar/) and a
shared popup primitive, instead of the v1 monolith. The rewrite ships **under
the existing names** — `DatePicker`, `data-coreui-date-picker`,
`.date-picker` — so no renaming is needed on your side, but the options below
changed. The v1 shells are gone; if you need to compare against the old
behaviour, v5 is the reference. Mapping:

- **Removed — projected regions replace button configuration.** `footer`,
  `todayButton`, `todayButtonClasses`, `cancelButton`, `cancelButtonClasses`,
  `confirmButton`, `confirmButtonClasses`, `ranges`, `rangesButtonsClasses` →
  put a `<template data-coreui-template="footer">` /
  `data-coreui-template="ranges"` child in the picker element. Zero-argument
  actions bind with `data-coreui-picker-action="today|clear|reset|close"`;
  actions with arguments (range presets) go through `picker.getContext()`
  (`setDate`, `setRange`, `clear`, `reset`, `close` + read-only state).
- **Removed — obsolete with section fields.** `inputDateFormat`,
  `inputOnChangeDelay`, `inputReadOnly`, `placeholder` — the masked field is
  always a valid partial date; there is no free-text parsing to debounce.
  Section placeholders come from the section fields' own per-section options —
  `dayPlaceholder`, `monthPlaceholder`, `yearPlaceholder` and the rest.
- **Removed — `previewDateOnHover` and `separator` on the date range picker.**
  The fields no longer preview the hovered day in a second, read-only input;
  the calendar still highlights the range up to the hovered day. The separator
  between the two fields is always rendered, and `separatorIcon` /
  `separatorIconRtl` replace its icon.
- **Kept.** `timepicker: true` still adds the time half to the date picker, now
  as a row of time selects under the calendar rather than a second panel beside
  it. Because the two halves own different parts of one value, a picker with
  time does not auto-close: picking a day keeps the time and picking a time
  keeps the day. `seconds` narrows what the time half offers and
  `selectionOptions` is its escape hatch.
- **Changed.** With `timepicker`, a `minDate` or `maxDate` that carries a time
  of day bounds the time on its day: the time selects disable the hours,
  minutes and seconds before `minDate` or after `maxDate` there, a time the
  panel sets beyond the limit (the current time when that day is picked) moves
  to the limit, and a typed time beyond it marks the field invalid. A limit
  without a time of day covers its whole day.
- **Kept.** Calendar passthrough (`locale`, `minDate`/`maxDate`,
  `disabledDates`, `selectionType`, `showWeekNumber`, custom cell renderers, …),
  `container`, `startDate`/`endDate`/`date`, `startName`/`endName`/`name`,
  `size`, validation states, the data API
  (`data-coreui-date-picker`), and the imperative API
  (`show`/`hide`/`toggle`/`clear`/`reset`).
- **Passthrough is by name.** Options that belong to the inner calendar or date
  input are forwarded automatically, so `data-coreui-selection-type`,
  `data-coreui-show-week-number` and `data-coreui-format` work on the picker
  element. `calendarOptions` / `inputOptions` remain as the programmatic escape
  hatch.
- **Every selection type has a matching mask.** Month and year selection narrow
  the mask to `MM/yyyy` and `yyyy`. Week selection renders the native
  `<input type="week">` presentation — a fixed localized label plus week and
  year sections (`Week 29, 2026`, `Tydzień 29, 2026`), where the year is the
  ISO week-numbering year. Quarter selection narrows to `QQQ yyyy`
  (`Q4 2026`). The v1 text input rendered `2026W27`/`2026Q1`; the submitted
  value now follows the mask (set `format="yyyy-Www"` for an ISO-style
  field), and the event carries the v1-formatted string in
  `event.formattedDate` next to the `Date` in `event.date` (see "Picker events
  can be cancelled" below). The format parser gained `ww`
  (ISO week) and `q`/`QQQ` (quarter) tokens, plus quoted literals
  (`'Week' ww` — fixed text that may contain token letters, `''` escapes a
  quote).
- **Format comes from the mask.** `inputDateFormat` is replaced by `format`
  (forwarded to the field); `inputDateParse` still applies to pasted text.
- **Footer buttons are projected, `today`/`now` actions keep the v1 gating.**
  A projected button opting into the `today` action (`now` on the time picker)
  is disabled at initialization when its target falls outside
  `minDate`/`maxDate`/`disabledDates` — matching the v1 built-in button. This
  is one-way: the picker only ever sets `disabled`, never re-enables. The
  state of any other projected button is yours — the slot context exposes
  `isDateSelectable(date)` (`isTimeSelectable` on the time picker) for
  computing it. Programmatic dates (`setDate`, actions, the initial `date`
  config) run through the same min/max validation as typed ones: out of
  range, the field turns `is-invalid`, `errorChange` fires, and `getDate()`
  reports `null`. `dateChange` on the shells always carries the validated
  value, and `setDate()` does not fire it.
- **The calendar is built on first open, not at construction.** It accounts for
  ~83% of a date picker's DOM (and ~89% of a range picker's), and it is not
  observable before the popup opens, so the shells create it in `show()` and
  seed it from the field values. Measured over 20 pickers: date picker
  **65 ms → 15 ms** and **3200 → 540** DOM nodes, range picker **93 ms → 12 ms**
  and **6060 → 740** nodes. The first open costs a few milliseconds more (the
  calendar is built then); later opens are unchanged. Code reaching into the
  private `picker._calendar` before the first open now finds `null`.
- **`setRange()` and `reset()` on the Date Range Picker show the range in the
  calendar**, on the month of its start date (of its end date when there is no
  start), in whatever view the calendar is in and also when the range does not
  change, so a preset clicked again after browsing brings its range back. The
  calendar used to move only when a field changed, and went to the month of
  the end date when both changed. `clear()` leaves it where it is; `setDate()`,
  `today()` and `reset()` on the Date Picker already showed their date.

#### The pickers take their validation state from `validationState`

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

`invalid` and `valid` on `DatePicker`, `DateRangePicker` and `TimePicker` are
deprecated aliases of `validationState: 'invalid'` and `'valid'`, and
`validationState` also takes a state from `$form-validation-states`. A given
`'invalid'` now blocks the submit, and the next change of the value the user
makes (a pick in the panel, the cleaner, a footer or custom-range button that
changes the value, typing in the field) drops it; closing the popup and a
change your code makes keep it. An `.is-invalid` or `.is-valid` class on the
picker element is read once, when the picker starts. An `aria-invalid` on it is
not read at all; remove it, since the date range picker makes its element a
group and the attribute would be announced on that group. Set the state after
start with `setValidationState()`. See the Validation sections of the
[date picker](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/date-picker/#validation),
[date range picker](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/date-range-picker/#validation) and
[time picker](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/time-picker/#validation).

#### The date picker adopts the markup you write

The date picker is composed from the date input, the calendar and a popup,
joined by the one date it owns. Mark a `div` with `data-coreui-picker-field`,
a button with `data-coreui-picker-toggle` or `data-coreui-picker-cleaner`
inside the picker element and it uses them instead of generating its own —
a trigger can be a regular button with text, the icons can be plain `<svg>`
in the HTML. Nothing changes for the bare `<div data-coreui-date-picker>`.

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
`aria-expanded` moved from the picker's root element to the toggle button,
where it describes the control that opens the calendar; the toggle also
carries `aria-controls` and `aria-haspopup="dialog"`. Styles keyed to the
root's `.show` class are unaffected.

#### The date range picker is built on the date range input

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

The range picker no longer builds its two fields itself: it composes the new
[date range input](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/date-range-input/), which owns both dates, their
validation and the arrow between them. Two consequences:

- **The picker element is no longer the frame.** It wraps one:
  `<div class="date-picker date-range-picker picker"><div class="form-control-group form-date-range">…`.
  CSS or scripts that reached for `.form-control-group` on the picker's own
  element now find it one level down; `.show`, `.date-range-picker` and the
  `data-coreui-toggle` attribute stay where they were. `aria-expanded` moved
  from the element to the toggle button, which also carries `aria-controls`
  and `aria-haspopup="dialog"`.
- **The two array options are split in two**, following the rule that every
  label is its own option: `ariaLabels: ['Start date', 'End date']` becomes
  `ariaStartLabel` / `ariaEndLabel`, and `floatingLabels: ['Check-in',
  'Check-out']` becomes `startFloatingLabel` / `endFloatingLabel`.

  ```diff
  - data-coreui-floating-labels='["Check-in", "Check-out"]'
  + data-coreui-start-floating-label="Check-in" data-coreui-end-floating-label="Check-out"
  ```

#### Picker events can be cancelled, and `dateChange` has one shape

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`dateChange` / `startDateChange` / `endDateChange` on the Date Picker and
  Date Range Picker carry the same payload whichever way the date arrived.**
  `event.date` is the validated `Date` (or `null`) the field holds, and
  `event.formattedDate` is that value in the shape of `selectionType`. A
  selection from the calendar used to put the formatted value in `event.date`
  and the `Date` in `event.dateObject`, while a date typed into the field put
  the `Date` in `event.date` — the same listener saw two shapes.

  ```diff
  - const date = event.dateObject ?? event.date
  + const date = event.date
  ```

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The pickers emit their change events only when the value changes.** A
  pick of the value already selected, and `clear()` or `reset()` when the
  picker already holds that value, no longer fire `dateChange`,
  `startDateChange`, `endDateChange` or `timeChange`; v5 fired them on every
  pick and on every `clear()` and `reset()`. The new `setDate()`,
  `setTime()`, `setStartDate()`, `setEndDate()` and `setRange()` fire no change
  event at all.

- **`shown` and `hidden` on the pickers wait for the popup's transition**, like
  the modal, dialog, toast and collapse events: `shown` fires once the panel
  has finished opening (and not at all when it closes first), `hidden` once it
  has finished closing and left the page (and not at all when it reopens
  first). v5 fired both as soon as `show()` or `hide()` ran.

- **`show` and `hide` can be cancelled** on the Autocomplete, Multi Select,
  Date Picker, Date Range Picker and Time Picker, the way
  they already could on Modal and Dialog: `event.preventDefault()` in a `show`
  listener keeps the panel closed, in a `hide` listener keeps it open, and the
  matching `shown` / `hidden` does not fire. `hide` on a panel that is not
  shown no longer emits anything.

#### Date Input / Time Input

- **The `disabled` option is the only switch.** A `.disabled` class written on
  the field does not disable it: the field sets the class from the option.
- **`reset()` restores the date the field was created with**, not the last one
  passed to `update()`, and emits `dateChange` when the value moves. A native
  form reset (`form.reset()`, a reset button) now resets the field too.
- **RTL keyboard navigation now follows the visual direction in nested RTL
  contexts.** Arrow keys (and `Home`/`End`) mirror when the field renders
  right-to-left; the direction is read from the element's computed style
  instead of `document.documentElement.dir`, so a `dir="rtl"` ancestor works
  and not only a document-level direction.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **A required field blocks the form.** The value moved from an
  `<input type="hidden">`, which the browser leaves out of validation, to a
  `<textarea>` laid invisibly over the field, with `tabindex="-1"` and
  `aria-hidden="true"`; a textarea, unlike a text input, leaves Enter
  submitting a form that has no submit button. A `required` field that is
  empty, incomplete or holds a rejected date now stops the submit, and the
  browser puts focus on its first empty section with its own message over the
  field. The field marks itself invalid whenever a validation reports it, also
  outside a submit, and its value field fires `input` and `change` when the
  date changes. This applies to the date and time pickers and to both ends of a
  date range as well. Code that selects `input[type="hidden"]` inside the field
  needs `.form-date-time > textarea`.

#### Time Picker

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The `roll` infix is gone from the class and token names.** It marked which
  of two renderings a body used, and the time picker has one now, so
  `.time-picker-roll-col` and `.time-picker-roll-cell` are `.time-picker-col`
  and `.time-picker-cell`, with `--cui-time-picker-col-*` and
  `--cui-time-picker-cell-*` in place of their `-roll-` spellings.
  `.time-picker-roll` itself was always present on `.time-picker-body` and its
  declarations moved there, so the modifier is gone rather than renamed;
  `--cui-time-picker-body-padding` went with it, since the modifier zeroed the
  padding it set.

- **`seconds` governs the field as well as the panel, and both are on by
  default, as they were in v5.** v5 formatted its value with seconds, so
  `10:30:00 AM` is what both majors submit. `seconds="false"` takes the panel
  column and the field's seconds section away together; with the section present
  the value is complete only once the seconds are typed, the same as every other
  section of the field.

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`hours` is 0 to 23 in every locale.** v5 read the list and the filter in the
  column's cycle, so on a locale whose v5 columns were 12-hour (the ones that
  write AM and PM in Latin letters, such as `en-US`) `hours: [1, 3, 5, 7]` meant
  1, 3, 5 and 7 in the morning and in the afternoon, `12` meant noon and
  midnight, and a filter received 1 to 12. The 12-hour column still lists 1 to 12
  next to AM/PM, but each entry now stands for the 24-hour hours the list
  allows: picking an hour that only one period allows sets that period, and the
  period the picked hour does not allow is disabled. To keep such a list, name
  both halves: `[1, 3, 5, 7, 13, 15, 17, 19]`, with `0` and `12` for `12`. More
  locales get 12-hour columns than in v5, because the columns follow the field's
  clock, Korean, Traditional Chinese, Arabic and Mexican Spanish among them;
  their v5 columns were 24-hour, so a list written for them keeps its meaning.

- **The day period column speaks the locale.** It read "AM" and "PM" in every
  locale; it now shows the names the field shows, 오전 and 오후 in Korean. A
  longer name widens its column, so `--cui-time-picker-cell-width` is now the
  narrowest a cell gets, and `--cui-time-picker-cell-padding-x` keeps the name
  off the cell's edges.

- The time selection body is now a shared primitive used by both pickers, so the
  `hours`/`minutes`/`seconds` filters and the 12/24-hour locale split behave
  identically in each. See
  [time picker](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/time-picker/) and the date picker's own
  [time half](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/date-picker/#with-time).
- **The time row sits under the calendars again, with its clock icon.** v6.0.0-alpha
  put it beside them in a column of its own; `.date-picker-body` is a grid with
  the v5 areas once more, so `.date-picker-timepickers` spans the calendars it
  belongs to, with its clock icon back as an inline SVG that takes its colour
  from `--cui-date-picker-time-icon-color`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The date picker's time controls carry its own name.** `.time-picker-inline-icon`
  and `.time-picker-inline-select` are `.date-picker-time-icon` and
  `.date-picker-time-select`, with `--cui-date-picker-time-icon-*` and
  `--cui-date-picker-time-select-*` in place of the `--cui-time-picker-inline-*`
  tokens. They sit in the date picker's panel and are styled from its stylesheet,
  so nothing about them belonged to the time picker but the name. The time
  picker's own panel is untouched.
- **The time selection is one tab stop, and the arrows move between its parts.**
  v6.0.0-alpha gave a tab stop to every column of the roll, so Tab walked the
  hour, minute, second and AM/PM lists one by one. The body is a composite now,
  like the section field it mirrors: <kbd>Tab</kbd> reaches it once,
  <kbd>Arrow Left</kbd> and <kbd>Arrow Right</kbd> move between the parts and
  stop at the ends, and <kbd>Arrow Up</kbd> / <kbd>Arrow Down</kbd> still change
  the focused value. The date picker's time selects answer the same keys.
- **Each picker has one time rendering, and the `variant` option is gone.** The
  time picker shows the roll; the date picker's time half shows the selects,
  which is what v5 rendered inside a date picker with time. Each picker builds
  its own rendering, so the choice is no longer an option anywhere;
  `selectionOptions` still reaches the body a picker built, for the hour and
  minute filters and the labels. The date picker's time selects moved
  with it: it now ships in the date picker stylesheet, so a page that only uses
  the time picker no longer downloads it.
- Same removals as the date pickers: `footer`/`cancelButton`/`confirmButton` and
  their class options give way to a projected
  `<template data-coreui-template="footer">` with
  `data-coreui-picker-action="now|clear|reset|close"`; `inputReadOnly`,
  `inputOnChangeDelay` and `placeholder` are obsolete with a section field.
- **`reset()` restores the value the picker was constructed with.** In v2 the
  shell remembers it: setting a value goes through the field's `update()`, which
  rewrites the field's own configuration, so the field can no longer restore the
  original on its own.

#### The v1 picker shell classes are gone

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

The rewritten pickers style the field through
`forms/_form-date-time.scss` and render their icons as inline SVG, so the v1
shell's own elements no longer exist: `.date-picker-input`,
`.date-picker-input-wrapper`, `.date-picker-input-preview`,
`.date-picker-cleaner`, `.time-picker-input` and `.time-picker-cleaner`.
Custom CSS targeting those needs rewriting against
the new structure; everything else (`.date-picker`, `.date-picker-popup`,
`.date-picker-body`, `.date-picker-footer`, the `-sm`/`-lg` size modifiers, the
`.time-picker-*` bodies) keeps its name.

#### The picker panel is `.date-picker-popup` / `.time-picker-popup`

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

`-dropdown` was the vocabulary of the per-family panels, and every other family
lost it when the options menu became `.combobox-popup`. The pickers were the
last holdout:

| v5 | v6 |
| --- | --- |
| `.date-picker-dropdown` | `.date-picker-popup` |
| `.time-picker-dropdown` | `.time-picker-popup` |

The rename comes with the reason for it. **The panel declares its own tokens
now** rather than inheriting them from the field's root through a
`*:not(.date-picker) > …` patch, so nothing inside it depends on having a
particular ancestor — it renders the same in place, teleported through the
`container` option, or rendered into `document.body` by a framework port's
portal.

`width: min-content` went with it: an absolutely positioned panel already
shrinks to its content, and both pickers measure identically without the
declaration.

#### `.form-select` is absorbed by `.form-control`

- **A `<select>` takes `.form-control` now**, like every other control. The two
  were already the same thing everywhere but the chevron: measured across every
  computed property, `.form-select` and `.form-control` on the same `<select>`
  differed in exactly ten — the eight `background-*` that draw the chevron and
  the padding that makes room for it. Everything else already came from the
  shared `--cui-control-*` tokens.
- **`<select class="form-control">` used to render without any arrow at all** —
  `.form-control` sets `appearance: none`, which removes the native one, and
  nothing put ours back. That is fixed by the same change.
- **New: `.form-control-caret`** puts the chevron on an element that is not a
  `<select>` — a button that opens a listbox, for instance.
- **`.form-select`, `.form-select-sm` and `.form-select-lg` keep working** as
  aliases of the `.form-control` classes, not as a second implementation.
  Deprecated in v6, removed in v7.

  ```diff
  - <select class="form-select form-select-lg">
  + <select class="form-control form-control-lg">
  ```

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The `$form-select-*` Sass variables are gone**, along with
  `scss/forms/_form-select.scss`. Most were pass-throughs to `$input-*` /
  `--cui-btn-input-*` and their `.form-control` equivalents apply
  unchanged; the chevron's own are renamed `$form-control-select-*`. The runtime
  tokens move with them: `--cui-form-select-bg-img` →
  `--cui-control-select-bg-img`, `-bg-position`, `-bg-size`,
  `-indicator-padding` → `--cui-control-select-padding-end`, and the
  `-feedback-icon-*` pair.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The chevron is translucent, and its color is no longer a Sass variable.**
  `$form-control-select-indicator-color` and its dark counterpart are gone; the
  SVG carries `#00000080` and `#ffffff80`, and the control declares both images
  as `--cui-control-select-bg-img` and `--cui-control-select-bg-img-dark`, dark
  mode switching which one the `background-image` reads. A color inside a data
  URI cannot read a token, so making it translucent is what lets the chevron sit
  correctly on a field of any color instead of on the two we picked. Measured on
  the default field, the stroke goes from `rgb(44, 49, 60)` to `rgb(127, 127,
  127)` in light mode and from `rgb(223, 223, 223)` to `rgb(132, 133, 135)` in
  dark. Override the token with your own data URI to get a solid one back.
- **Two differences a select inherits from `.form-control`,** neither of which
  changed a rendered box in any case measured. It paints its background with
  `background-clip: padding-box`, the value `.form-control` has always used to
  avoid a rounded-corner artefact in Firefox — 0.12% of the control's pixels, the
  antialiasing of the four corners. And `.form-select-sm` / `-lg` now carry the
  `min-height` their `.form-control` counterparts always had, so a sized select
  is finally the same height as a sized input; it only shows if you have cut the
  padding below that floor.

#### Form labels carry their own weight

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.form-label` and `.col-form-label` render medium and full-contrast by
  default.** `$form-label-font-weight` is `var(--cui-font-weight-medium)`
  (was `null` — labels inherited the surrounding weight) and
  `$form-label-color` is `var(--cui-fg-body)` (was `null`), so a label inside
  a muted or tinted context keeps the page's text colour instead of fading
  with it. Set the knobs back to `null`, or `--cui-label-font-weight` /
  `--cui-label-color` to `inherit`, for the old behaviour.

#### Validation: `data-coreui-validate` replaces `.was-validated`

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
Client-side validation no longer uses `.was-validated` or the bare `:valid` /
`:invalid` pseudo-classes. Add `data-coreui-validate` to the `<form>` instead:
the styles ride the `:user-invalid` pseudo-class, so feedback appears only
after the user interacts with a control or attempts to submit — no JavaScript
toggles a class anymore, and after an AJAX submission there is nothing to
remove to reset the form's appearance.

- **Success styling is opt-in.** `data-coreui-validate` alone shows only
  invalid styling; set `data-coreui-validate="valid"` to also colour the
  valid fields.
- **Server-side validation is unchanged** — `.is-invalid` / `.is-valid` keep
  working globally, without the attribute.
- **Feedback of a check, a radio, a switch, a `.form-control` or `.form-select`
  in an input group, or a control placed directly in a component frame belongs
  to the [`.form-field`](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/field/) around it.** The field shows it while
  the control carries the state, wherever it sits in the field, for example in
  a `.form-field-content` next to the label, and a check, a radio or a switch
  colours the label. Feedback right after any other control still follows it.

```diff
- <form class="needs-validation" novalidate>
+ <form data-coreui-validate novalidate>
```

The starter JavaScript is gone. A form with `data-coreui-validate` and
`novalidate` is handled by the new [form plugin](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/validation/#usage): it
stops an invalid submit, marks the invalid controls with `.is-invalid` and
`aria-invalid="true"`, links the message of each to it with `aria-describedby`
while it stays invalid, and moves focus to the first. Remove the copied
script; to show the errors your own way, call `preventDefault()` on
`validate.coreui.form`. Components follow the attribute:
the stepper marks the controls of a `novalidate` step form itself each time
it validates the step, passing or not: `.is-invalid` on the invalid ones (and
`.is-valid` on the valid ones under `data-coreui-validate="valid"`), and the date / time / date-time inputs key their submit-time state off the
attribute instead of the class.

The stepper also sets `aria-invalid` on those controls and moves focus to the
first of them, and its panes are no longer `aria-live`: while a control is
invalid, the stepper adds its `.invalid-feedback` to `aria-describedby` instead,
the one the stylesheet shows for it (after it, or in the `.form-field` around
the controls the [field page](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/field/) lists) or the one named in
`data-coreui-invalid-feedback`, so the message is read with the field. The panes used to read out every message that appeared in them;
a message that changes while the user types is now silent, so pass the ones
that have to be heard to [`announce()`](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/getting-started/accessibility/#announcing-messages).
Going back to an earlier step no longer validates the current one.
`stepValidationComplete.coreui.stepper` now fires before the stepper decides,
as `validate.coreui.form` does, so a validity its handler sets with
`setCustomValidity()` blocks the step, and `event.isValid` tells whether every
control passed before the handler ran. `next()` and `finish()` fire it once
per step instead of twice.

#### Form components share one validation state

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
The multi select, the chip input, the combobox, the OTP input, the date and
time fields, the pickers and the autocomplete take and show their validation
state the same way, described under
[Form components](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/validation/#form-components):

- **The state comes from the `validationState` option**, or from `.is-invalid`
  or `.is-valid` on the component's element, read once when the component
  starts. A class or an `aria-invalid` written on the element later is not
  read; set the state with `setValidationState()`.
- **A given `'invalid'` blocks the submit**, with the text of the component's
  invalid feedback as the browser's message, until the user changes the value
  or the form is reset, which removes the state.
- **The form plugin and the stepper leave the field that carries a component's
  value to the component**, which shows the state they report on its frame.
- **After taking the result over** with `preventDefault()` on
  `validate.coreui.form`, keep `event.isValid` rather than calling
  `checkValidity()`: the call reports every invalid field again, and the
  components then show their error.

#### Stepper: `next()` on the last step finishes the stepper

`next()` on the last enabled step now does what `finish()` does: it validates
the step and, when it passes, finishes the stepper and fires
`finish.coreui.stepper`. It used to validate the step and stop, so a wizard
driven by a single Next control never finished. A step button with
`.disabled` does not count, so `next()` finishes on the step before it.

#### Stepper: `finish()` before the last step does nothing

`finish()` called on an earlier step used to move to the next step, the same
as `next()`. It now does nothing until the last enabled step is active, the
same as in React and Vue. Call `next()` to move forward.

#### Stepper: the active indicator's background follows the theme

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

`--cui-stepper-step-indicator-active-bg` is removed. The active step's
indicator background is a 5% tint mixed on the step from the theme colour, or
from `--cui-stepper-step-indicator-active-color` without one, so a `.theme-*`
class on the stepper or on a single step button tints it along with the text
and border. To give the active indicator another background, set
`--cui-stepper-step-indicator-bg` on `.stepper-step-button.active`.

#### Input group: `.has-validation` removed

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
`.has-validation` is gone. It existed so a group could keep its corner logic
with a `-feedback` element sitting inside it as the last child — in v6 the
feedback lives **outside** the group: wrap the group in a
[`.form-field`](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/field/) and put the `-feedback` after it, and the
message renders under the whole frame. A group still carrying the class simply
gets the default corner handling; a group still carrying the feedback *inside*
should move it out.

```diff
- <div class="input-group has-validation">
-   <span class="input-group-text">@</span>
-   <input type="text" class="form-control is-invalid" required>
-   <div class="invalid-feedback">Please choose a username.</div>
- </div>
+ <div class="form-field">
+   <div class="input-group">
+     <span class="input-group-text">@</span>
+     <input type="text" class="form-control is-invalid" required>
+   </div>
+   <div class="invalid-feedback">Please choose a username.</div>
+ </div>
```

#### Input group: one frame class instead of five

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

`.input-group` squared off the frame of a field component by naming each
family's wrapper — `.autocomplete-input-group`, `.date-picker-input-group`,
`.time-picker-input-group`, `.form-multi-select-input-group` and
`.form-password`. All five became `.form-control-group`, so the input group
targets that one class:

```diff
- > .autocomplete:not(:last-child) > .autocomplete-input-group,
- > .date-picker:not(:last-child) > .date-picker-input-group,
- > .time-picker:not(:last-child) > .time-picker-input-group,
- > .form-multi-select:not(:last-child) > .form-multi-select-input-group,
- > .form-password:not(:last-child) > .form-control,
+ > .form-control-group:not(:last-child),
```

A field component *is* the frame — it carries `.form-control-group` on its own
root rather than nesting one — so it is a direct child of the input group like
any other control, and there is no wrapper level to reach through.

If you wrote CSS against those wrapper classes to adjust a field inside an
input group, target `.form-control-group` instead. A hand-authored group — a
password field, a chip input — is now a first-class child of `.input-group`
and needs no wrapper of its own.

**The input group styles ship in the `components` layer**, not `forms`. It
assembles controls rather than being one, and the layer it sat in lost to
`components` whatever the selector said — so a `.btn` inside a group kept the
radius it sets for itself and showed rounded corners against the field. If your
own stylesheet re-squared those corners as a workaround, the override is no
longer needed. A rule of yours that relied on the group losing to a component
now loses to the group instead; give it a layer of its own if you need it back.

#### Chip Input keeps focus among the chips after a removal

Removing the focused chip used to send focus back to the text field. It now
moves to the next chip, else the previous one, and only reaches the text field
once no chip is left, the same rule Chip Set follows, so several chips can be
removed in a row from the keyboard.

#### Chip Input is submitted only once you name it

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

The hidden input that carries the chips took a generated `name` when the option
was not set, so a form carried a key nobody chose. It is named only when you
ask for one, and the option table no longer lists the name as auto-generated.
`dispose()` also removes that input, where it used to leave it in the page.

#### Chip Input carries its chips in a `<textarea>`

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

The chips travel in a hidden `<textarea>` instead of an `<input type="hidden">`,
because the browser leaves a hidden input out of validation. The submitted value
is the same, the chips separated by commas, and the `id` and `name` options land
on the `<textarea>`. A script or a selector that looked for
`.chip-input input[type="hidden"]` looks for `.chip-input > textarea` instead.
With it the chip input takes `required` and `validationState`, blocks the submit
while it is empty and required, and a native form reset puts back the chips it
started with. A disabled chip input is no longer submitted, like any disabled
field. See [Validation](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/chip-input/#validation).

#### Chip Input takes the frame class itself

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

```diff
- <div class="form-control-group chip-input" data-coreui-chip-input>
+ <div class="chip-input" data-coreui-chip-input>
```

A chip input has no control to wrap — the element *is* the frame — so it adds
`.form-control-group` to itself rather than asking you to write both. Writing
both still works.

#### Chip Input reads the group's tokens

`.chip-input` no longer keeps a private copy of the frame. `--cui-chip-input-min-height`
and `--cui-chip-input-gap` are gone: the element reads `--cui-control-min-height`
and `--cui-control-group-gap` from `.form-control-group` like every other group,
and `.chip-input-sm`/`-lg` switch those tokens instead of private ones. The Sass
variables that only re-pointed a `--cui-btn-input-*` token (`$chip-input-min-height`,
`-font-size`, `-bg`, `-color`, `-border-width`, `-transition-*` and the size
variants of min-height and font-size) are removed; what remains is what the chip
input sets differently — padding, border colour and radius, and the gap, per
size. The disabled chip's opacity reads `--cui-control-group-disabled-opacity`
instead of a literal `.65`. Rendered values are unchanged.

#### Number Input (new)

- **New component: [Number Input](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/number-input/)** (PRO) — a number field
  with a stepper you can actually hit. The browser's own spinner is tiny, shows
  only on hover in some engines and does not exist at all on iOS; the component
  replaces it with two buttons sized like every other adornment, on the shared
  [`.form-control-group`](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/form-control-group/) frame. Nothing to migrate —
  it is listed here because the `number-input` name, its classes and its Sass
  variables are new in v6.

#### The `disabled` option is the only switch of the form components

Combobox, Autocomplete, Multi Select, Chip Input, Chip Set, Chip, Rating, Date
Input and Time Input take their disabled state only from the `disabled` option
(`data-coreui-disabled="true"`). A `.disabled` class in the markup, or a
`disabled` attribute on the select a multi select enhances or on a combobox
toggle button, does not disable them. All but Chip Set set the class from the
option, Combobox and Multi Select also that attribute; Chip Set passes the
option to its chips. `dispose()` leaves the class and the attribute for the
next instance to write again from its options. In v5 a `.disabled` class disabled a chip, a chip set and
a chip input, and the autocomplete data API skipped an element with the class;
write `data-coreui-disabled="true"` instead, on a single chip too. A
`disabled` class returned by `chipClassName` no longer disables a chip.

A disabled multi select now disables its select, so the form leaves it out of
validation and of the submitted data, as it does for the other disabled
fields; a `required` multi select that is disabled no longer blocks the
submit.

#### Multi Select

- **The option checkboxes are the standard form-check surface.** The indicators
  used to re-implement the form-check visuals as `::before` pseudo-elements with
  their own variable set; they are decorative `.form-check-input` spans now
  (`aria-hidden`, since `aria-selected` carries the semantics), so size, border,
  radius, background and theming all come from the form-check surface and the
  two stay in lockstep. The per-indicator look variables are gone with
  them — only `$combobox-option-indicator-width` survives, and it follows the
  checkbox: `--cui-check-size` where the indicator can see it, and the shared
  `--cui-control-size` otherwise. Theme the option checkbox through `$check-*` /
  `--cui-check-*` instead.
- **Validation state comes from `validationState`, and the select is no longer
  watched.** `invalid` and `valid` are deprecated aliases of
  `validationState: 'invalid'` and `'valid'`, and `validationState` also takes a
  state from `$form-validation-states`. A given `'invalid'` now blocks the submit,
  and the next change the user makes to the selection drops it. An `.is-invalid`
  or `.is-valid` class on the select is read once, when the component starts, and
  moves to the frame; a class or `aria-invalid` written on the select later is not
  read, so set the state with `setValidationState()`. A native form
  reset restores the selection the control started with. See
  [Validation](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/multi-select/#validation).
- **<kbd>Backspace</kbd> in the empty search box focuses the last chip.** It
  removed the last value at once; the first press now moves the focus to the
  last chip and the second removes it, as in Chip Input. The arrow keys move
  between the search box and the chips, the selection is one tab stop, removing
  a focused chip moves the focus to its neighbour, and a value removed through a
  chip, <kbd>Backspace</kbd> or the cleaner is announced (`ariaRemovedAnnouncement`).
  A single select and the `counter` and `text` selections still remove the last
  value at once.
- **`selectionType: 'chips'` is the default, and `'tags'` is deprecated.** The
  two render the same chips; `'tags'` keeps working until v7.
  `ariaTagDeleteLabel` is deprecated in favour of `ariaChipRemoveLabel`; the old
  option still names the remove button and takes precedence while it is set.

#### Password Input and Number Input build their own frame

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

Both components supply their own buttons, so they supply the frame that lays
them out. The markup is a plain form control carrying the toggle:

```diff
- <div class="form-control-group">
-   <input type="password" class="form-control">
-   <button type="button" class="form-control-action" data-coreui-toggle="password" aria-label="Toggle password visibility">
-     <span class="form-password-action-icon"></span>
-   </button>
- </div>
+ <input type="password" class="form-control" data-coreui-password-input>
```

- **`data-coreui-toggle` moves from the button to the input**, and its value is
  the component's name: `password-input` (and `number-input` for the new
  [Number Input](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/number-input/)). The button no longer exists in your
  markup, so there is nothing left to put it on.
- **Classes you write on the input, other than `.form-control`, move onto the
  frame** — size, spacing, width. They describe the field, and once the input is
  wrapped the field is the frame; a margin left on the control would sit inside
  the border. They move back on `dispose()`.
- **An input already inside a `.form-control-group` is left where you put it**,
  so a field can share a frame with other adornments, and only a frame the
  component created is removed on `dispose()`.
- The accessible name of the toggle is the `ariaToggleLabel` option now, since
  the button is no longer yours to label.

#### Password Input: the toggle icon comes from JavaScript

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

The toggle button carried `<span class="form-password-action-icon"></span>`,
and CSS painted it as a mask that swapped through a sibling selector. The
plugin renders it now — inline SVG on `currentColor`, like every other
adornment in the library — so the button needs no icon markup at all:

```diff
  <div class="form-control-group">
    <input type="password" class="form-control">
-   <button type="button" class="form-control-action" data-coreui-toggle="password" aria-label="Toggle password visibility">
-     <span class="form-password-action-icon"></span>
-   </button>
+   <button type="button" class="form-control-action" data-coreui-toggle="password" aria-label="Toggle password visibility"></button>
  </div>
```

- `.form-password-action-icon` is gone, and `scss/forms/_form-password.scss`
  with it. That takes **2.5 kB of URL-encoded SVG out of the stylesheet** — it
  was there for every page, whether or not one had a password field.
- `$form-password-icon-show` / `$form-password-icon-hide` and the matching
  `--cui-form-password-icon-*` properties are gone. Supply your own artwork
  through the new `showIcon` / `hideIcon` options instead, the way the other
  adornments already take theirs. Both are sanitized; `allowList`, `sanitize`
  and `sanitizeFn` control that.
- The plugin now initializes on `DOMContentLoaded` for markup carrying
  `data-coreui-toggle="password"`, so the icon is present without any script of
  your own — the same data-API pass Chip, Chip Set, Chip Input and Search
  Button already make.

#### Password Input: the toggle is disabled with the field

The toggle button used to stay enabled on a disabled field and ignore the
click, while `toggle()` flipped a disabled field anyway. The button is now
disabled with the field and leaves the tab order, like the other action
buttons, and neither the button nor `toggle()` changes what a disabled field
shows: a password revealed before the field is disabled stays visible, and
can be hidden once the field is enabled again.

#### Icons rendered by JavaScript

- New components render their icons as **inline SVG on `currentColor`** (the
  chips pattern) instead of CSS `mask` data-URIs: swap them with the
  `indicatorIcon` / `separatorIcon` options (sanitized with the SVG allowlist),
  recolor them with plain CSS `color`. The corresponding
  `$date-picker-*-icon`/`-icon-size` Sass variables disappear with the v1
  shells.
- **The calendar navigation icons moved to JavaScript too.** The four
  `$calendar-nav-icon-{prev,next,double-prev,double-next}` Sass variables are
  **removed**, along with the CSS custom properties and mask rules that painted
  them. Customize them with the new `navPrevIcon` / `navNextIcon` /
  `navPrevDoubleIcon` / `navNextDoubleIcon` calendar options instead (inline SVG,
  sanitized). `$calendar-nav-icon-width/-height/-color/-hover-color` stay — the
  icon inherits `color`, so recoloring is plain CSS.
- **`Calendar`'s default `allowList` is now `SVGAllowlist`** (was
  `DefaultAllowlist`), so the component can render its own inline SVG icons —
  and custom cell renderers may return SVG as well. Strictly more permissive;
  scripts and event handlers are still stripped.
- **Directional icons are resolved in JavaScript, not CSS.** v1 swapped the
  range separator with a second Sass variable
  (`$date-picker-separator-icon-rtl`) applied through an RTL mask; v2 picks
  between `separatorIcon` and `separatorIconRtl` when it renders, based on the
  element's computed direction — so a `dir="rtl"` ancestor works, not just a
  document-level direction. The calendar mirrors its prev/next icons the same
  way. **Consequence for custom code:** a component that resolves directional
  icons at construction time must be in its final DOM position when it is
  created — a detached element has no computed direction (the nearest ancestor
  that declares `dir` answers for it, `dir="auto"` included, and the document
  after that, but placement first is the reliable path).

#### Date and time fields are submitted only once you name them

<span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>

Date Input and Time Input took an `id` written on the host as
permission to submit, naming their hidden input `<component>-<id>` when no
`name` was configured, so `<div data-coreui-date-input id="delivery">` posted
`date-input-delivery` — a key the page never asked for, from an attribute a
`<label for>` needs anyway. Date Picker and Time Picker
inherit the rule through the field they embed, and with `floatingLabel` that
field is given a generated id, so they posted a key that changed on every load
(`date-input-date-picker-190423`). The value field is named from `name` alone
now, and a field without one stays out of the form. `dispose()` removes the
sections, the separators and the value field, takes back the `aria-label` it
generated, and gives the host the author's own child nodes, the same nodes,
not copies. It used to leave a live-looking
field behind that kept submitting.

#### Range / Range Slider

- **Changed.** **A pressed thumb takes `--cui-primary-bg-muted`**, the theme's
  `bg-muted` under a `.theme-*` class, instead of a 30% tint of the primary
  colour (`$form-range-thumb-active-bg`).
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **A Range Slider handle with no name of its own is no longer submitted.** An
  array of names shorter than the handle count gave the leftover handles the
  literal name `undefined`, so a form carried an `undefined=` key. Those handles
  are unnamed now, as they already were in the React and Vue editions.
- **The filled track, value tooltip and tick marks come from a `Range` plugin.**
  Wrap the input in `<div class="form-range">` and give the input
  `.form-range-input`; the plugin writes `--cui-range-fill` (`0`–`1`) on the
  wrapper and the CSS draws the fill from it, `data-coreui-tooltips` adds a
  value tooltip on hover, drag and focus, or permanently with `"always"` (the
  same `tooltips`/`tooltipsFormat` options as the range slider), and a linked `<datalist>` renders tick
  marks laid out on a grid. Every such wrapper initializes itself. The input
  fires `changed.coreui.range` with the numeric `value`. The fill is the
  theme colour at half strength, a shade lighter than the thumb. **The bare
  `<input class="form-range">` keeps working unchanged** as the CSS-only form of
  the control; it gets no plugin, no fill and no instance. New tokens on
  `.form-range`: `--cui-range-track-fill-bg`, `--cui-range-track-disabled-bg`
  and the `--cui-range-tick-*` family (labelled ticks use `-height`, unlabelled
  ones `-minor-height`).
- **The range track sits halfway between `--cui-bg-2` and `--cui-bg-3`**
  (`color-mix(in oklch, …)`, was `--cui-bg-3`), in both the native range and
  the range slider. `--cui-bg-3` and the `secondary` colour
  resolve to the same grey in light mode, so a `theme-secondary` thumb and
  fill vanished against the track.
- **Validation reaches the range.** `.is-invalid`/`.is-valid` on the input
  (either form) colour the thumb and its focus ring with the state, and a
  feedback element after the wrapper toggles through `:has()`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The range slider renders the markup of the range.** It builds
  `<div class="form-range">` inside its element, with one native
  `.form-range-input` per handle, so it looks like a range and reads its
  tokens; the slider keeps the handles in order, moves them and draws the band
  itself. The class names
  follow: `.range-slider` → the inner `.form-range`, `.range-slider-input` →
  `.form-range-input`, `.range-slider-vertical` → `.form-range-vertical`,
  `.range-slider-tooltip` → `.form-range-tooltip` (now a `<div>`),
  `.range-slider-ticks`/`.range-slider-tick` → `.form-range-ticks`/
  `.form-range-tick` with the text in `.form-range-tick-label`, and the
  per-tick `.clickable` → `.form-range-ticks-clickable` on the container.
  `.range-slider-inputs-container` and `.range-slider-track` are gone (the
  first input draws the track), and so is the `.disabled` class on the element
  (the inputs are disabled); the stylesheet styles none of the
  `.range-slider-*` classes any more. The handles no longer carry `role="slider"`,
  `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuemax` or `aria-valuenow`: the native inputs expose
  them. Options, events and methods keep their names, apart from `ariaLabels`
  (below); the `changed.coreui.range` event that an inner Range fired on each
  handle is gone, so listen to `input.coreui.range-slider` and
  `change.coreui.range-slider` on the slider. The range tokens are
  declared on that inner `.form-range`, so a `--cui-range-*` token set on the
  slider element itself (inline, or by a class on it) no longer reaches the
  handles: target its `.form-range` (`.my-slider .form-range { … }`).
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The range slider's `ariaLabels` option is `ariaLabel`.** It takes an
  array with one name per handle, as before, a comma-separated string, or a
  function `(index, total) => string`, the shape the OTP input uses for its
  digits; `data-coreui-aria-labels` becomes `data-coreui-aria-label`.
- **The range slider element is a group named after the field.** The
  component gives it `role="group"`, so an `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` on
  it names the group, and a screen reader announces it when the focus enters
  the slider. With several handles, each handle carries only its own name
  ("Minimum value"); a single handle takes the name of the group, followed by
  its `ariaLabel` when it has one. `aria-describedby` moves onto every handle.
  A `role` written on the element is replaced with `group`.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The range slider takes a validation state.** The new `validationState`
  option, or `.is-invalid`/`.is-valid` on the slider element read once when it
  starts, shows on the element and on the handles, and a given `'invalid'`
  blocks the submit through the first handle until the user moves a handle or
  the form is reset. A class written on the element later is not read; set
  the state with `setValidationState()`. See
  [Validation](https://coreui.io/bootstrap/docs/forms/range-slider/#validation).
- **Range slider interaction follows the native control.** Pressing a handle no
  longer moves it a step; a press on the track or a clickable tick moves the
  nearest handle and it follows the pointer (mouse, touch or pen), with
  `change.coreui.range-slider` on release instead of on press; the events carry
  numbers; a form reset restores the configured values instead of the middle of
  the range; `setValue()` and `dispose()` touch only what the slider built, so
  content of the element stays.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The range slider's handles and track read the range's tokens.** The
  `--cui-range-slider-thumb-*` and `--cui-range-slider-track-*` families are
  gone; the slider's `.form-range` declares the `--cui-range-*` family
  instead, so one setting styles both controls. Rename: `--cui-range-slider-thumb-{width,
  height,bg,border,border-radius,box-shadow,active-bg,disabled-bg}` →
  `--cui-range-thumb-*`, `--cui-range-slider-thumb-transition` →
  `--cui-range-thumb-transition-{property,duration,timing}`,
  `--cui-range-slider-track-{width,height,cursor,bg,border-radius,box-shadow}`
  → `--cui-range-track-*`, `--cui-range-slider-track-in-range-bg` →
  `--cui-range-track-fill-bg`, `--cui-range-slider-disabled-track-in-range-bg`
  → `--cui-range-track-disabled-bg`. The Sass variables follow
  (`$range-slider-thumb-*`, `$range-slider-track-*` → `$form-range-*`).
  `--cui-range-slider-label-*`, `--cui-range-slider-tooltip-margin-*`
  and `--cui-range-slider-vertical-*` are gone too, with their Sass variables
  (`$range-slider-label-*`, `$range-slider-tooltip-margin-*`,
  `$range-slider-vertical-track-*`): tick labels read `--cui-range-tick-label-color` and `-font-size`, the tooltips
  sit like the range's, a vertical slider's height is
  `--cui-range-vertical-height` and its track width `--cui-range-track-height`;
  the tick label padding has no counterpart. The filled band keeps its half-strength mix of the theme colour, now
  shared with the range's fill, and a disabled slider greys the band through
  the theme slot on the track.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The range slider's `labels` option is `ticks`.** Every entry draws a tick
  mark, with or without text, so the option and everything around it carry the
  name the `Range` plugin uses: `labels` → `ticks`, `clickableLabels` →
  `clickableTicks` (`data-coreui-ticks`, `data-coreui-clickable-ticks`),
  `.range-slider-labels-container` → `.form-range-ticks`,
  `.range-slider-label` → `.form-range-tick`, and `--cui-range-slider-label-*`
  → `--cui-range-tick-label-*`. The entry shape is unchanged: a string, a
  bare number, or `{ value, label, class, style }`.
- **Range slider ticks sit on a grid.** The plugin builds
  `grid-template-columns` (rows when vertical) from the gaps between tick
  values, so ticks are in normal flow: the container sizes itself, and the
  resize listener plus the measured container height are gone. Every tick
  draws its mark from `--cui-range-tick-*` — taller when it has text — and
  the ticks are sorted by value. A bare number in `ticks` is a tick without
  text at that value. The new `list` option reads a `<datalist>` like the
  range does and merges its options with `ticks`. Custom CSS that
  positioned `.range-slider-tick` through `left`/`bottom` no longer applies.
- **Range slider tooltips follow the range's modes.** `tooltips: true` shows a
  handle's tooltip on hover, drag and visible focus; `"always"` keeps them on.
- **A theme beats `--cui-range-thumb-bg`, as everywhere else.** The token carried
  the `--cui-theme-*` slot inside its own default, so setting it inside a themed
  container dropped the theme. The slot sits on the property now.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The value bubble is a tooltip.** The plugin renders
  `<div class="form-range-tooltip tooltip bs-tooltip-top">` (vertical:
  `bs-tooltip-start`, or `bs-tooltip-end` in a right-to-left page) with
  `.tooltip-arrow` and `.tooltip-inner`, so the
  bubble looks like every other tooltip on the page — dark on a light page —
  and follows `--cui-tooltip-*` and the theme slots. The twelve
  `--cui-range-slider-tooltip-*` look tokens (`-bg`, `-color`, `-padding-*`,
  `-font-size`, `-border-radius`, `-box-shadow`, `-arrow-*`, `-max-width`,
  `-transition`, `-zindex`) and their Sass variables are gone, and so are
  `-margin-bottom` and `-margin-end`. The tooltip declares its own
  `--cui-tooltip-*` tokens, so set them on it: for the previous light bubble,
  give the slider `tooltipClass: 'light-tip'` and declare
  `--cui-tooltip-bg: var(--cui-bg-4)` and `--cui-tooltip-color: var(--cui-fg-body)`
  on `.light-tip`.

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The pressed thumb adapts to the color mode.** Its color was 70% white
  mixed into the theme color at build time, so in dark mode the pressed
  thumb flashed a near-white pastel. It now mixes toward `--cui-body-bg` at
  runtime — identical in light mode, dark and correct in dark mode.
- **Both sliders are themeable**: the filled track and the thumbs read the
  theme slots, so a `.theme-*` class recolors them; without one they stay
  on primary as before.

#### Sidebar

- **Every `--cui-sidebar-*` token comes from a Sass map** (`$sidebar-tokens`,
  `$sidebar-nav-tokens`, `$sidebar-narrow-tokens` and the variant maps), so the
  sidebar takes `@use ... with ()` overrides like every other component. The
  narrow variant's variables (`$sidebar-narrow-*`) live in
  `sidebar/_sidebar-narrow.scss`.
- **Hover and active links read the foreground ladder.** Their text was the
  pure black/white `--cui-emphasis-color` and their background the
  Sass-computed `--cui-tertiary-bg-translucent`; they are `--cui-fg-body` and
  `color-mix(in srgb, var(--cui-fg-body) 10%, transparent)` now — a tint that
  works on any surface and can be retuned in the browser. Slightly stronger
  than before on a light sidebar, slightly weaker on a dark one.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`--cui-tertiary-bg-translucent` is gone from `:root`.** The sidebar was
  its only reader. Set `--cui-sidebar-nav-link-hover-bg` /
  `-active-bg` on `.sidebar-nav` for a different tint.
- **Theme variants.** `.sidebar.theme-{color}` paints the surface, text,
  icons and the hover/active links from that colour's slots. `.sidebar-light`
  joins `.sidebar-dark` as the scheme switch.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.sidebar-body` and `.sidebar-form` are removed.** Neither carried
  styles beyond the sidebar's own padding. `.sidebar-self-hiding-*` had already
  gone in v6 — a sidebar that hides itself below a breakpoint is `hide()` from
  a resize handler, or the mobile mode the sidebar takes below `lg` on its own.
- **The mobile backdrop reads the shared backdrop tokens.** `.sidebar-backdrop`
  mixes `--cui-backdrop-color` at `--cui-backdrop-opacity` and blurs by
  `--cui-backdrop-blur`, like the dialog and the drawer; `--cui-sidebar-backdrop-bg`
  carries the mix and `--cui-sidebar-backdrop-blur` the blur, and
  `--cui-sidebar-backdrop-opacity` (`.5`, the old fade target) is gone. The
  `.backdrop-*` helpers work on the sidebar too.
- **`.sidebar-narrow` gets its bottom padding back.** It read
  `--cui-sidebar-toggler-height` from a token declared on the toggler, a
  descendant, so the padding silently fell to 0; the token is declared on
  `.sidebar` now.

#### ScrollSpy

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The active link is chosen by an activation line, not by scroll
  direction.** The plugin used to compare each section's offset with the
  previous scroll position, so the same scroll offset could highlight a
  different link depending on which way you arrived. It now activates the
  lowest section whose top edge has crossed a line drawn `topMargin` below
  the top of the scroll container — a pure function of the scroll position.
  Two visible consequences: **the last active link stays highlighted while a
  gap between sections crosses the line** (the navigation no longer goes
  blank), and **a short last section is activated when the container is
  scrolled to the bottom**, even though its top never reaches the line.
- **New option `topMargin`** (default `12%`) places the activation line; it
  takes a percentage of the container's height or a pixel value (`96px`).
  A pixel line is recomputed on resize.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`rootMargin` defaults to `null` and is now the raw override.** Set it and
  it is handed to the `IntersectionObserver` unchanged, taking precedence over
  `topMargin`; a v5 configuration such as `rootMargin: '0px 0px -25%'` keeps
  working as written. `threshold` defaults to `[0]` instead of
  `[0.1, 0.5, 1]` — with an activation line a section only needs to report
  whether it crosses it.
- **`smoothScroll` finishes the navigation.** Once the scroll settles, the
  URL hash is replaced with the link's target and focus moves to the section.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **The deprecated `offset` option is removed** — it has been a thin alias
  since the IntersectionObserver rewrite. Use `topMargin` instead:
  `offset: 100` &rarr; `topMargin: '100px'`.

#### Tab

- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **Constructing a Tab outside a tab-panel parent now throws a `TypeError`**
  instead of silently returning a half-initialized instance, matching upstream.
  The silent variant hid real markup mistakes: the instance existed but never
  activated anything.
- The `data-coreui-toggle="pill"` and `"list"` toggles **stay** — they are
  documented markup, and v6 keeps documented markup working.
- **`$nav-link-transition` splits into
  `$nav-link-transition-property`/`-duration`/`-timing`**, emitted as
  `--cui-nav-link-transition-*`. The link's transition was compiled into its rule
  while `.tab-pane` in the same file already read the three-token form, so half
  the file could be retimed in the browser and half could not. Setting
  `--cui-nav-link-transition-duration` now takes effect; before it was ignored.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.nav-link` is a flex row, and it fills on hover and when active.** The link
  lays its children out with `--cui-nav-link-gap`, `--cui-nav-link-align` and
  `--cui-nav-link-justify`, so an icon and its label line up without a wrapper.
  It also takes `--cui-nav-link-hover-bg` and `--cui-nav-link-active-bg`, rounded
  by `--cui-nav-link-border-radius` — the base nav used to change text color
  alone. Set the two backgrounds to `transparent` to keep the old look.
  Under a theme, hover reads `--cui-theme-fg` and the lighter
  `--cui-theme-bg-subtle`, and the active item the darker `--cui-theme-bg-muted`,
  so hovering a link never outweighs the selected one.
  `.nav-tabs`, `.nav-underline` and `.nav-underline-border` square the link off,
  and the two underline variants take neither fill, hover or active: they mark
  their state with a line, and a radius curves that line's ends while a fill says
  the same thing twice.
- <span class="badge fg-emphasis-danger bg-subtle-danger">Breaking</span>
  **`.nav-tabs` draws its line with an inset `box-shadow`, not a border.** The
  links no longer pull themselves over the container's border with a negative
  `margin-bottom`; each link carries its own bottom border (the line colour at
  rest, the panel colour when active) and the container paints the line as
  `box-shadow: inset 0 calc(-1 * var(--cui-nav-tabs-border-width)) 0
  var(--cui-nav-tabs-border-color)`. The rendered result is pixel-identical,
  but custom CSS targeting `.nav-tabs`' `border-bottom` or the links' negative
  margin needs a second look; recolour the line through
  `--cui-nav-tabs-border-color`. `.card-header-tabs` compensates with a deeper
  negative margin instead of zeroing the border.
- **`--cui-nav-gap` is new**, declared on `.nav` and defaulting to `0`, so the
  base nav renders exactly as it did. It gives the token a home on the component
  whose name it carries: `.navbar-nav` already overrode it, and `.nav-underline`
